# Śrī Navadvīpa-dhāma-māhātmya

*and*

# Śrī Navadvīpa-bhāva-taraṅga

*Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur*

*Śrī Chaitanya Sāraswat Maṭh*

---

## About this edition

The text is that of Śrī Chaitanya Sāraswat Maṭh’s English edition of 2015 (translated from the Bengali edition of
1988). Here only the English prose translation of each verse is given, with the verse numbers of that edition;
where the edition gives one translation for two verses, the number is a range (e.g. 15–16).

---

## Translator’s Note

I bow at the feet of Śrī Gurudev, the Vaiṣṇavas, and the all-merciful Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nityānanda. I place this book in their hands and pray that it pleases their hearts.

## Content

This edition of *Śrī Navadvīpa-dhāma-māhātmya* and *Śrī Navadvīpa-bhāva-taraṅga* has been prepared from Śrī Chaitanya Sāraswat Maṭh’s Bengali publications of these texts and includes translations of their publisher’s notes. A Bengali poem by Śrīla Bhakti Sundar Govinda Dev-Goswāmī Mahārāj which invites everyone to join Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām Parikramā sets the tone of the book. Preceding the primary texts is the preface from Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur’s original publication of *Śrī Navadvīpa-dhāma-māhātmya* in 403 Chaitanyābda (1888 AD), as well as his poem *Śrīman Navadvīpa-dhāma-vandanā*, which summarises the entire *Śrī Navadvīpa-dhāma-māhātmya* in twenty Sanskrit verses.

Following *Śrī Navadvīpa-dhāma-māhātmya* and *Śrī Navadvīpa-bhāva-taraṅga* is a set of excerpts from Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur’s writing and an overview of the glories of Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām compiled from lectures by Śrīla Bhakti Rakṣak Śrīdhar Dev-Goswāmī Mahārāj. The list of the ten offences to the Holy Dhām, a glossary, and the map of Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām prepared by Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur have been included at the back.

## Translation

The texts of *Śrī Navadvīpa-dhāma-māhātmya* and *Śrī Navadvīpa-bhāva-taraṅga* are presented in a four-fold format: 1) the Bengali text of each verse, 2) its Roman transliteration, 3) its English prose-ordered phrase-by-phrase gloss, and 4) its English prose translation. In this edition, punctuation marks to indicate grammatical compounds, speech, and past active participles have been inserted into the Bengali text and its transliteration to aid comprehension. When reading the prose-ordered phrase-by-phrase gloss—a style of translation known as *anvaya*, which is common in Bengali translation of Sanskrit—please note: 1) past active participles are often conjugated in tandem with the finite verb they accompany, 2) verb tenses are often translated to suit idiomatic usage in English (e.g. present tense and present perfect tense are often translated as simple past tense), 3) idiomatic uses of the passive voice in Bengali are translated in the active voice in English, and 4) correlative pronouns and prepositional cases are typically translated in the simplest grammatically equivalent forms possible. It is our hope that this method of presentation will aid readers in appreciating the simplicity and sweetness of the Bengali text itself.

## Origin

This book has been prepared under the auspices of Śrī Chaitanya Sāraswat Maṭh’s Present-Sevāite-Āchārya, Om Viṣṇupād Śrīla Bhakti Nirmal Āchārya Mahārāj. Śrīpād Bhakti Rañjan Madhusūdan Mahārāj oversaw the production of the book and edited the final manuscript. Śrīpād Bhakti Prapanna Tīrtha Mahārāj and Lalita Mādhava Prabhu greatly assisted with translation. Śrī Viśākhā Devī Dāsī provided invaluable assistance, and numerous other devotees contributed with editorial suggestions and corrections. Śrīmān Purujit Prabhu financed the printing. We are indebted to all these devotees for their gracious contributions.

## Purpose

Śrīman Mahāprabhu’s prophecy that Hari-nām-saṅkīrtan would spread to every town and village of the world; Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur’s prophecy that people from all over the world would come to Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām and join the Bengalis in chanting the glories of Śrī Gaurasundar; and Śrīla Bhakti Siddhānta Saraswatī Ṭhākur’s prophecy that Westerners would learn Bengali just to study the writings of the Rūpānuga sampradāya’s Āchāryas are becoming fulfilled more and more gloriously with each passing year. It is our hope that this publication will serve to further all three of these expressions of divine will. May souls all over the world read the glories of Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām in this book and be inspired to continue the saṅkīrtan of Śrī Gaurasundar wherever they may be. May they be inspired to travel to Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām and perform parikramā in the company of devotees from Bengal and all over the world. May they pause their joyous saṅkīrtan from place to place throughout the Dhām to recite the glories of the Dhām as revealed by Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur. May they thus taste the nectar in the life of a surrendered soul:

> *gaura āmāra ye saba sthāne*  
> *karala bhramaṇa raṅge*  
> *se saba sthāna heriba āmi*  
> *praṇayi-bhakata-saṅge*  
> *gaura-vihita kīrtana śuni’*  
> *ānande hṛdaya nāche*  
> (Śaraṇāgati: 31.3–4)

“In the company of loving devotees, we visit the places where our Śrī Gaurasundar performed Pastimes, and as we hear the kīrtan prescribed by Him, our hearts dance with joy.”

On the disappearance day of  
Śrīla Śrīnivās Āchārya,  
10 November 2013

Humbly,  
Bhakti Kamal Tyāgī  
Śrī Chaitanya Sāraswat Maṭh  
Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām

> *nindantaṁ pulakotkareṇa vikasan-nīpa-prasūna-chchhaviṁ*  
> *prordhvī-kṛtya-bhuja-dvayaṁ hari harīty uchchair vadantaṁ muhuḥ*  
> *nṛtyantaṁ drutam aśru-nirjhara-chayaiḥ siñchantam ūrvī-talaṁ*  
> *gāyantaṁ nija-pārṣadaiḥ parivṛtam śrī-gaura-chandraṁ numaḥ*  
> (Śrī Rādhā-rasa-sudhā-nidhi: 1)

“His raised bodily hairs surpass the beauty of blooming kadamba flowers. With arms upraised, He loudly calls out, ‘Hari!’ ‘Hari!’ again and again. He dances nimbly and showers the surface of the earth with streams of tears as He sings surrounded by His associates. Let us glorify Śrī Gaurachandra.”

## Publisher’s Note

*Translated from the 1988 Bengali edition of*  
**Śrī Navadvīpa-dhāma-māhātmya**

By the boundless grace of our great-grand Gurudev, Om Viṣṇupād Śrīla Ṭhākur Bhakti Vinod, our long-standing desire to publish *Śrī Navadvīpa-dhāma-māhātmya* has been fulfilled. Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām is Gupta Vṛndāvan [Hidden Vṛndāvan]. Circumambulating the thirty-two miles of Śrī Vṛndāvan Dhām is a deeply cherished devotional practice for greatly enthusiastic devotees, yet Śrī Gaura’s devotees circumambulate Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām for the satisfaction of Śrī Śachī Nandan Gaurahari with even more enthusiasm and attain eternal good fortune. This circumambulation takes place unfailingly every year in an organised way from the chief original Maṭh—Śrī Chaitanya Maṭh, Śrī Chaitanya Sāraswat Maṭh, and other ashrams. In particular, assembled devotees from all corners of the world participate in the circumambulation of Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām arranged by Śrī Chaitanya Sāraswat Maṭh—which was established by Śrīla Śrīdhar Dev-Goswāmī Mahārāj in Kolergañj, Nabadwīp—and attain the highest fortune. By their earnest request, and for the benefit of all devotees who circumambulate the Holy Dhām, we have published this holy book which bestows the highest fortune of revelation of the Holy Dhām; we are thus greatly fortunate.

Śrīla Narottam Ṭhākur has written,

> *śrī-gauḍa-maṇḍala-bhūmi yebā jāne chintāmaṇi*  
> *tāra haya vraja-bhūme vāsa*  
> (Prārthanā: 39.3)

[“One who knows the holy land of Gauḍa to be made of wish-fulfilling gemstone resides in the land of Vraja.”]

The true form of the Dhām manifests only in the hearts of fortunate souls who are established, by the grace of Śrī Guru, in the spiritual land of dedication with proper sambandha-jñān [understanding of their relationship with the Lord]. Śrīla Rūpa Goswāmī Prabhu’s verse *ataḥ Śrī-Kṛṣṇa-nāmādi*[^00-publishers-notes-1] clearly teaches us this principle. So, awakening and increasing our propensity for dedication (sevā) is our one and only necessity, and this is possible only by attaining the grace of Śrī Guru and the sādhus. Thus, our one and only duty is to adhere to the practice of living a life of resolute faith and subservience to Śrī Guru and the sādhus.

> *dīkṣā-kāle bhakta kare ātma-samarpaṇa*  
> *sei kāle kṛṣṇa tāre kare ātma-sama*  
> *sei deha kare tāra chid-ānandamaya*  
> *aprākṛta-dehe tā̐ra charaṇa bhajaya*  
> (Śrī Chaitanya-charitāmṛta, Antya-līlā, 4.192–193)

[“At the time of initiation, devotees fully surrender themselves, and Kṛṣṇa accepts them. Kṛṣṇa makes their body spiritual and joyful, and in that supramundane body they serve Kṛṣṇa’s feet.”]

The Lord’s Name, Form, Qualities, Pastimes, Associates, and Glory are all supramundane. Only by Śrī Guru’s grace can a surrendered soul see the Lord’s supramundane Dhām with eyes free from māyā and attain the fortune of service there. May the nature of the Holy Dhām—*chintāmaṇi-prakara-sadmasu kalpa-vṛkṣa-lakṣāvṛteṣu surabhīr abhipālayantam* [“the abode made of wish-fulfilling gemstone covered by millions of wish-fulfilling trees where Śrī Govinda tends His cows”]—be realised by reading this book. This is my earnest prayer at the holy feet of Śrī Guru, the Vaiṣṇavas, and the Lord of the Dhām, Śrīman Mahāprabhu. Enough verbosity.

Publisher  
The humble and fallen,  
Śrī Bhakti Sundar Govinda  
(in the pen name of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Śaraṇ Brahmachārī)

First, I faithfully offer my obeisance to our society’s present Āchārya, Parivrājakāchārya Śrīmad Bhakti Sundar Govinda Dev-Goswāmī Mahārāj, the dearmost associate of our beloved Gurudev, Om Viṣṇupād Śrīla Śrīdhar Dev-Goswāmī Mahārāj, by whose mercy our endeavour to print and propagate *Śrī Navadvīpa-bhāva-taraṅga* by Śrī Gaurāṅga’s associate Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur has been possible. Our Śrīla Guru Mahārāj has said that the influence of māyā will decrease to the extent that Ṭhākur Bhakti Vinod’s books are preached throughout the world. If we are able to provide true help to even one conditioned soul in this way, then the true ideal of kindness towards others (*jīve dayā*) will be realised. His divine message is our only prospect and hope. May this endeavour satisfy the devotees and please Ṭhākur Śrīla Bhakti Vinod as well. This is my prayer.

Humbly,  
The publisher,  
Śrī Prapanna Kṛṣṇa Brahmachārī  
(Śrīpād Bhakti Prapanna Tīrtha Mahārāj)  
11 March 1988

## Śrī Navadvīpa-dhāma-parikramāya Āhvāna

An invitation to Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām Parikramā

**Śrīla Bhakti Sundar Govinda Dev-Goswāmī Mahārāj**

Originally published in *Śrī Gauḍīya Darśan*:  
Volume 1, Issue 8, Sunday, 11 March 1956

**1.** You do not have to wander any longer in this vile world, riding along the path of death in the chariot of this temporary body within the forest of material existence, which is filled with ferocious tigers. You do not have to wander any longer in this vile world.

**2.** Pursuing the desires of youth and getting married, you have known the ‘vow of household life’ in name only. Continuing in this way birth after birth, circling about in household life, have you realised the land of Māyā is covered with thorns?

**3.** Not only that, your mind has made arrangements to continue your wandering from country to country, and from village to village; but your desires have never been fulfilled. And still you have built a residence—a mere temporary inn which lasts but a few days—upon this earth?

**4.** Hey! How much longer will you wander in the darkness of Mahāmāyā’s illusion in that temporary home? Your wife, sons, and family are worthless, temporary, and vile. Will you find a jewel within a swamp, trench, or rubbish heap?

**5.** Clasping your feet, I tell you this essential truth: you do not have to wander any longer. There is not one drop of the water of hope here. There is no hope of any solace but only cries of distress here. The burdens of material existence are like a blazing fire.

**6.** You do not have to wander any longer in the forest of material existence. The devotees of Gaura call everyone: “Hey, listen!

**7.** “Men and women, young and old, come immediately! Come to the divine abode made of wish-fulfilling gemstone, the land of Śrī Gaurāṅga’s advent! Come and circumambulate this land in the company of loving devotees during the most auspicious time of your life: the day of Śrī Gaurāṅga’s advent.

**8.** “Circumambulate the divine abode and your wandering throughout this universe for innumerable births will end, the disease of material existence will go away, all your suffering will be dispelled, and you will experience Goloka on this earth.” So call the devotees of Gaura.

**9.** You do not have to wander any longer in this vile world.

---

**Śrīla Śrīdhar Mahārāj:** Those days have gone by, when I guided the parikramā, singing and dancing in the front. I was mad when I guided the parikramā: I became almost mad to sing and dance in the Name of Gaurāṅga. I would do so almost without any fatigue. Those days have gone by. Now I cannot go.

**Devotee:** We can get a palanquin. Then you can come with us.

**Śrīla Śrīdhar Mahārāj:** Gauraharibol, Gauraharibol, Gauraharibol … I am pleased to think that so many of you will dance somewhere in Gaura Maṇḍal taking the Name. That makes me more proud. We feel more proud to think that so many souls are adding to the glorious opulence of Gaura Dhām, as foretold by Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur: “The day is not far off when the devotees from the Western countries will mix with the Eastern devotees, and they will chant and dance together in the Name of Gaurasundar and Nityānanda.”

## Preface

A translation of the preface to the original publication of  
*Śrī Navadvīpa-dhāma-māhātmya* written by  
**Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur**

“As there are books about the glory of other Dhāms, so there should be a book about the glory of Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām”—long ago this desire arose in the hearts of the sādhus. But because such a book cannot be published until Śrīman Nityānanda Prabhu desires it, it has remained unpublished. Now that a suitable time has arrived by the inspiration of Śrīman Nityānanda Prabhu, and with the sanction of the Vaiṣṇava sādhus, I have published this book.

Using the descriptions of the Dhām in Śrīman Narahari Dās’s *Bhakti-ratnākara* and *Navadvīpa-dhāma-parikramā-paddhati*, and those made by Śrī Narottam Ṭhākur, Śrī Paramānanda Dās, and other writers as a basis, I have visited all the villages within the thirty-two mile circumference of Śrīman Nabadwīp Dhām, collected verbal accounts from knowledgeable residents of those places, gathered all the clues about the Dhām within the scriptures, and then composed this book.

In scriptures such as the Vedas and Purāṇas, there are numerous clues about Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām. Upon reading the verse from *Śrīmad Bhāgavatam* (7.9.38) cited below, it is easily understood that the scriptures have also described the hidden Avatār (Śrī Chaitanya Mahāprabhu) in a hidden way:

> *dharmaṁ mahāpuruṣa pāsi yugānuvṛttaṁ*  
> *chhannaḥ kalau yad abhavas triyugo ’tha sa tvam*

[“O Supreme Lord! You protect the dharma in accordance with each age. Because You are hidden in the Age of Kali, You are known as Triyuga, He who appears in three ages.”]

The small map included in this book was prepared from a government authorised topographic map. Therefore, it should be considered accurate. As the map’s scale is small, only the names of the principal places have been given.[^00-preface-1]

---

> *navadvīpaḥ sākṣād vraja-puram aho gauḍa-paridhau*  
> *śachī-putraḥ sākṣād vraja-pati-suto nāgara-varaḥ*  
> *sa vai rādhā-bhāva-dyuti-suvalitaḥ kāñchana-chchhato*  
> *navadvīpe līlāṁ vrajapura-durāpāṁ vitanute*  
> (Śrī Navadvīpa-śatakam: 79)

“Aho! Nabadwīp in Śrī Gauḍa Maṇḍal is Vṛndāvan itself. The son of Śachī is Kṛṣṇa Himself, the son of King Nanda and the best of paramours. Enveloped in the heart, halo, and golden lustre of Śrī Rādhā, He reveals Pastimes in Nabadwīp which are unattainable in Vṛndāvan.”

[^00-preface-1]: *The map referred to here can be found at the back of the book.*

## Śrīman Navadvīpa-dhāma-vandanā

A summary of *Śrī Navadvīpa-dhāma-māhātmya* in twenty Sanskrit verses

**Śrīla Bhakti Vinod Ṭhākur**

**1.** The śruti known as *Chhāndogya* calls it ‘the city of the Supreme Absolute’. The smṛti calls it ‘Vaikuṇṭha’, the abode of Lord Viṣṇu. Others know it as ‘the white island’. The rare relishers of rasa call it ‘the forest of Vraja’. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**2.** In one of its regions, Brahmā, constantly repenting for his misbehaviour caused by bewilderment, worshipped Gaura, the ocean of mercy. The Lord then fulfilled the deepest desire in Brahmā’s heart. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**3.** In one of its regions, Bhagavatī, the golden-complexioned daughter of the Himalayas, the mother of the universe, beheld the unparalleled form of the object of her worship, the son of Śachī. She then placed the dust of the Lord’s feet upon the parting in her hair. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**4.** In one of its regions, the wielder of the thunderbolt, Lord Indra, remorseful for his impudence, took shelter of beautiful Surabhi, lovingly worshipped the feet of the Lord beside a tree, and attained a most auspicious boon from Gaura Himself. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**5.** In one of its regions, His heart attracted by their service, Gaura appeared to the seven sages at noon, and, oh, bestowed upon them a pleasing boon at the perfect time! I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**6.** In one of its regions, a brāhmaṇ chief, who longed for Puṣkara Tirtha but because of his old age lacked the ability to make the pilgrimage there, saw the supremely auspicious Puṣkara Tīrtha before him. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**7.** In one of its regions, the Lord of sacrifice, who bears the dazzling form of a boar, His heart was drawn by the power of His devotee’s service, affectionately bestowed upon His devotee the purest thing in the three worlds—love for Śrī Gaurāṅga. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**8.** In one of its regions, the king of the seasons, checking his own power, worshipped Śrī Chaitanya, who, along with His associates, was dancing in a grove whose creepers and shrubs, laden with fruits and flowers, bowed to Him. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**9.** In one of its regions, Jahnu Muni, upon seeing the Gaṅgā’s glistening waters approaching during his worship, drank her distracting waves. He then released her again as his daughter because of the devotion (of King Bhagīrath). I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**10.** In one of its regions, in the Tretā-yuga of a previous era, the son of Daśarath, the wealth of the sages, Rāma, with his other half, Sītā, and Lakṣmaṇ, resided for a few days on the bank of the Gaṅgā beneath a pleasure tree. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**11.** In one of its regions, the sage Nārad directly beheld the greatest of Gods, Nārāyaṇ, who is supremely worshippable to all, and then saw the Supreme Lord in His golden form. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**12.** In one of its regions, by the grace of Gaura, the Lord’s friend Arjuna, served by Draupadī and accompanied by his brothers, resided for a few days in a large, auspicious forest that is worshipped by hosts of sages. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**13.** In one of its regions, Rudra, lost in divine love, gently dances with his associates, enlivened by the sound of ḍamarus. Oh! How he continuously sings about Viśvambhar in this place! I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**14.** An abundance of holy places, surrounded by water, shine eternally throughout this land. Attended by Śiva, Brahmā, and all the other gods, these places destroy all sin and are worshippable to all the sages. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**15.** Here, Prauḍhā Māyā, the Lord’s warden, along with her husband, deceives the enemies of the Vaiṣṇavas by giving them material happiness and bewilders those who are blinded by pride in their futile study of the scriptures. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**16.** Here, Kālī, the wife of Śambhu and destroyer of the demons, sincerely protects the devotees of the Lord out of affection for them. Lost in divine love, she eternally worships Gaura in ecstasy. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**17.** Saraswatī, the goddess of speech, whose heart is given to the service of the Lord’s feet, eternally resides on the bank of the Gaṅgā in this land and personally bestows upon the brāhmaṇs knowledge of all the scriptures on conduct and other subjects. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**18.** Adorned with the lustre of Śrī Rādhā, the Lord appeared here from the womb of Śrī Śachī. Intent on purging the filth of Kali, He engaged in supremely purifying chanting of the Name, accompanied by His associates. I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**19.** Oh! Here, some devotees, expert in the service of the Lord, directly see with their own eyes Śrī Gaurāṅga dancing amidst His associates and their wives in this supremely beautiful city! I bow here to Nabadwīp, the abode of supreme joy.

**20.** One who resides in Nabadwīp without duplicity and recites this prayer with devotion at the time of worshipping the Lord, genuinely attains the supremely delightful joy of love for the Lord, the embodiment of spiritual ecstasy—the son of Śachī, who is Kṛṣṇa Himself.

---

# Chapter 1. The Glories of the Dhām

**1.** All glory to the son of Śachī, the moon of Nabadwīp! All glory to the avadhūt Nityānanda Rāy!

**2.** All glory to the worshippable Śrī Advaita Prabhu! All glory to Gadādhar Paṇḍit and Śrīvās Paṇḍit!

**3.** All glory to Nabadwīp Dhām, the best of all abodes! All glory to the residents of Nabadwīp, the associates of Śrī Gaura!

**4.** Bowing at the feet of all the devotees, I will briefly describe Nabadwīp Dhām.

**5.** The glories of Nabadwīp Maṇḍal are unlimited. Brahmā and the other gods do not know them. So, who can describe them?

**6.** Ananta Śeṣa is unable to describe them with thousands of mouths. So, how will I, a tiny soul, be able to?

**7.** It is true that the glories of Nabadwīp are unlimited. The god of gods, Lord Śiva, cannot reach their end.

**8.** Still, the will of the moon-like Śrī Chaitanya is all-powerful, and under its control, the devotees give orders.

**9.** By the will of Śrī Chaitanya, the devotees have ordered me, and so I describe the glories of Nadia by their mercy.

**10.** There is another very confidential subject, which I do not want to mention, yet cannot but mention.

**11.** When Śrī Chaitanya disappeared, He ordered the devotees to reveal His abode and Pastimes.

**12.** Of all Avatārs, our moon-like Śrī Chaitanya is the most confidential known in this world.

**13.** His confidential Pastimes are mentioned confidentially in the scriptures. They are not revealed in the hearts of non-devotees.

**14.** For a long time, Māyā Devī hid the confidential scriptures that existed about His Pastimes.

**15–16.** Māyā Devī covered the eyes of the scholars and at all times kept secret all the descriptions of Śrī Chaitanya throughout both the manifest and unmanifest scriptures.

**17.** When the deep Pastimes of Śrī Gaura became unmanifest, Māyā understood the will of the Lord and withdrew her deception.

**18.** Māyā lifted her veil from the eyes of the souls and revealed the truth about Śrī Gaura to the material world.

**19.** The hidden scriptures then appeared clearly and dispelled the doubts of all souls.

**20.** The extremely merciful Nityānanda Prabhu revealed the truth about Śrī Gaura in the hearts of the souls.

**21.** Receiving His order, Māyā removed her veil, and the wise devotees discovered the wealth of the scriptures.

**22.** Those whose doubts are not dispelled by this are unfortunate. Why do such persons meaninglessly continue their lives?

**23.** Whenever and howsoever the Lord bestows His mercy, the fortunate souls become extremely happy.

**24–25.** Everyone should know the symptoms of misfortune: to consider your own intelligence to be great and not accept the mercy of the Lord. Through false arguments, souls fall into Māyā’s traps again and again.

**26.** “O souls of Kali-yuga, come! Give up duplicity, and accept pure divine love for Śrī Gaurāṅga in full!”

**27.** Saying this, Nityānanda calls out again and again. Still, unfortunate souls do not accept.

**28.** Let us consider carefully why souls disregard divine love.

**29.** In various ways, all souls pursue happiness. In this world, some souls study logic, and others practise yoga.

**30.** For the sake of happiness, some leave home and go to the forest, and others wage wars between nations.

**31.** For the sake of happiness, some run after women and wealth, and others pursue the arts and sciences.

**32.** For the sake of happiness, some give up on happiness and learn to be miserable, and others drown themselves in the ocean.

**33.** Raising His arms, Nityānanda calls, “O souls! Give up the misery of exploitation and renunciation, and come with Me!

**34.** “You are searching for happiness. I will give that to you, and I will not take anything from you in return.

**35.** “You will not undergo any trouble, loss, or pain. Simply chant, ‘Śrī Gaurāṅga’ and dance without a care.

**36.** “The happiness I will give you is unparalleled. It is pure, eternal joy, free from all illusion.”

**37.** In this way, Nityānanda Prabhu begs everyone to accept divine love, but the unfortunate, as a result of their sins, do not want it.

**38.** The unlimited sins of anyone who once chants, “Gaurāṅga! Nitāi!”are destroyed.

**39.** Everyone, please listen to another confidential subject. The wealth of Gaura’s Pastimes is appropriate for the souls of Kali-yuga.

**40.** Gaurahari, as Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, plays with the sakhīs eternally in Vṛndāvan.

**41.** Through the scriptures, the souls understood the nature of Pastimes in Vraja, and the greatness of Vraja and Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa’s eternal Pastimes there.

**42.** Through the scriptures, the whole world understood the unlimited glories of Kṛṣṇa’s Name and Kṛṣṇa’s abode.

**43.** Still, in general, souls did not attain divine love for Kṛṣṇa. What is the reason for this? Consider it within your heart.

**44.** In this regard, there is a highly confidential subject that souls bewildered by Māyā do not consider.

**45.** When souls serve Kṛṣṇa for many births but do not develop divine love, then certainly they have made offences.

**46.** When souls, free from offences, chant the Name of Kṛṣṇa, they quickly attain divine love for Kṛṣṇa.

**47.** The special characteristic of Śrī Chaitanya’s Pastimes is that souls attain the wealth of divine love even while they commit offences.

**48.** Pure divine love for Kṛṣṇa searches for souls who call out “Nitāi-Chaitanya!”

**49.** Offences do not obstruct such souls, and their eyes cry with pure love for Kṛṣṇa.

**50.** Within a short time, offences leave on their own. The souls’ hearts become purified, and divine love develops within them.

**51.** The offences of the souls in Kali-yuga are unlimited and unavoidable. There is no deliverance for the soul without the Name of Gaura.

**52.** Thus, in Kali-yuga, I do not see any way without Gaura, and the scriptures proclaim this.

**53.** Gaurachandra appeared in Nabadwīp, the crown atop all the holy places.

**54.** In other holy places, offenders are punished, but in Nabadwīp offences are always forgiven.

**55.** The brothers Jagāi and Mādhāi are evidence of this. They committed offences but attained Nitāi and Chaitanya.

**56.** Brother! Let alone other holy places, offenders and demons are punished even in Vraja.

**57.** In Nabadwīp, souls who commit hundreds and hundreds of offences are easily delivered by the mercy of Nitāi.

**58.** The sages say that the land of Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām in Śrī Gauḍa Maṇḍal is most glorious.

**59.** Brother, souls who reside in Nabadwīp are very fortunate. They attain devotion to Kṛṣṇa.

**60.** Souls who once visit Nabadwīp are freed from all offences.

**61.** The scriptures say that by remembering Nabadwīp you attain that which pilgrims attain by visiting all the other holy places.

**62.** Souls who see Nabadwīp attain the wealth of divine love for Kṛṣṇa birth after birth.

**63.** Even souls who go to Nabadwīp with a materialistic motivation do not take a human birth again.

**64.** All the scriptures say that while walking through Nabadwīp, such souls attain the result of ten million horse sacrifices with each step.

**65.** Those who reside in Nabadwīp and chant their mantra—that holy mantra comes alive and easily delivers them.

**66.** That which yogīs attain after ten years in other holy places is attained in three nights in Nabadwīp.

**67.** The liberation attained through knowledge of Brahma in other holy places is attained in Nabadwīp simply by bathing in the Gaṅgā.

**68.** In Nabadwīp, even without knowledge of Brahma, seekers of liberation can attain residence in the Lord’s abode, a form like the Lord’s, opulence like the Lord’s, association with the Lord, and absorption into the Lord.

**69.** Enjoyment and liberation personified bow at the feet of the pure devotees in Nabadwīp and always wait upon them as maidservants.

**70.** Although devotees kick them and drive them away, these maidservants do not leave the feet of the devotees or run away.

**71.** Brother, by spending one night in Nabadwīp, souls attain that which is gained by residing in the seven principal holy places for one hundred years.

**72.** Thus, Nabadwīp Dhām is the best of all abodes. Souls who take shelter here in Kali-yuga are delivered.

**73.** The two forms of knowledge—that which liberates and that which nourishes—constantly and perfectly serve the residents of Nabadwīp.

**74.** Bhakti Vinod, whose aspiration is the shade of Nitāi and Jāhnavā’s feet, sings the glories of Nadia and rejoices.

---

# Chapter 2. The Nature and Dimensions of the Dhām

**1.** All glory to the son of Śachī, the moon of Nabadwīp! All glory to the avadhūt Śrī Nityānanda Rāy!

**2.** All glory to Nabadwīp, the best of all abodes! Who can describe its glory?

**3.** Nabadwīp Dhām shines eternally within Gauḍa Maṇḍal, and is served by Gaṅgā Devī.

**4.** Gauḍa Maṇḍal is one hundred and sixty-eight miles in circumference. Gaṅgā Devī continuously flows throughout it.

**5.** Gauḍa Maṇḍal is a one-hundred-petalled lotus, and at its centre is the extremely beautiful Nabadwīp.

**6.** In circumference, its whorl is ten miles in circumference, and its fragrant bloom is thirty-two miles.

**7.** Its outer petals form a one-hundred-petalled lotus that extends for one hundred and sixty-eight miles.

**8.** The circumference of Gauḍa Maṇḍal is that size. The scriptures state that its diameter is fifty-six miles.

**9.** Its radius, measured from its centre point, is twenty-eight miles.

**10.** The centre of Nabadwīp Dhām is the pure, spiritual Yoga Pīṭh.

**11.** Śrī Gauḍa Maṇḍal is made of wish-fulfilling gemstone. It is spiritual and joyful: everything within it is made of spiritual energy.

**12.** The water, land, trees, and so on, are all spiritual. Kṛṣṇa’s three energies exist there eternally.

**13.** The Dhām is a manifestation of the sandhinī aspect of the Lord’s personal energy.

**14.** The Dhām is the eternal site of the Lord’s Pastimes. It is a manifestation of the Lord’s inconceivable energy; it is not material.

**15.** Still, the conditioned souls are bewildered by illusion and see the Dhām to be material.

**16.** Eyes covered by clouds see that the sun is covered, but the sun is never actually covered by clouds.

**17.** Similarly, worldly people see the spiritual form of Śrī Gauḍa Maṇḍal to be a formation of matter.

**18.** But once Nityānanda blesses someone, they see the joyful, spiritual Dhām everywhere.

**19.** The Gaṅgā, Yamunā, and other holy rivers exist there eternally. The seven holy cities, Prayāg, and other holy places are present throughout the Dhām.

**20.** Śrī Gauḍa Maṇḍal is Vaikuṇṭha itself; fortunate souls see this clearly.

**21–22.** On the order of the Lord, Māyā, the shadow of His personal energy, extends her influence and covers the eyes of the averse souls. Thus, they do not see the glory of the Lord’s spiritual abode.

**23.** Souls who always reside in Śrī Gauḍa Maṇḍal are the most fortunate souls in this world.

**24.** The gods living in heaven see that they have beautiful four-armed forms with a cloud-like lustre.

**25.** The residents of the thirty-two miles of Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām have a golden lustre and are always engaged in chanting the Name.

**26.** From the heavens, Brahmā and the other gods worship the residents of Nabadwīp in various ways.

**27–28.** Brahmā says, “When will I attain the fortune of becoming a blade of grass in Nabadwīp and receiving the foot dust of the servants of Śrī Gaura’s feet?

**29.** “Alas! Gaurachandra deceived me and made me the emperor of the universe.

**30.** “When will my entanglement in karma be cut away? When will my heart give up its pride and become pure?

**31.** “When will my mentality of being a master be destroyed? When will I become a pure servant and attain the shelter of Śrī Gaura’s feet?”

**32.** The gods, sages, and Rudras live throughout Nabadwīp eternally.

**33.** They spend their lives performing austerities for long periods, but they do not receive the mercy of Nityānanda.

**34–35.** As long as their identification with the body does not go away and humility does not appear within their hearts, even Brahmā and Śiva do not attain the wealth of Gaura and Nitāi’s mercy, although they strive for it.

**36.** All these points will be described ahead. Brother! Faithfully and carefully listen.

**37.** Brother! Give up argument in these matters—inappropriate argument is very inauspicious.

**38.** The Pastimes of Śrī Chaitanya are a deep ocean. Argument over them, like a banana flower’s husk, is simply troublesome.

**39.** The endeavour of those who want to cross over this world by argument is fruitless. Such persons do not attain anything.

**40.** Those who forgo argument and follow the sādhus and scriptures quickly attain Śrī Chaitanya.

**41.** The śrutis, smṛtis, and tantras always sing the glories of Nadia on the order of Nityānanda Prabhu.

**42.** Read all these scriptures and honour the words of the sādhus; then you will understand Nabadwīp.

**43.** During the Age of Kali, all the holy places are very weak. Only Nabadwīp is greatly powerful.

**44.** By the will of the Lord, this holy place was unmanifest for a long time, and its greatness was not revealed.

**45.** When the influence of Kali strongly increased, the other holy places naturally became powerless.

**46.** The Supreme Lord reflected on this and made an arrangement for the welfare of all souls.

**47.** “Understanding a patient’s disease, a good doctor prescribes medicine. For a strong disease, he gives strong medicine.

**48.** “The Age of Kali has become severe, and the disease of the souls has become serious. Without strong medicine, I cannot check it.

**49–50.** “If I do not reveal the Abode, Name, and Form that I have kept completely hidden, then how will the souls be delivered?

**51.** “The souls are My servants, and I am their master. If I do not deliver them, then they will never be delivered.”

**52.** Thinking thus, Śrī Chaitanya appeared with His Name, His abode, and His servants.

**53.** This promise of the Lord is eternal: “I will deliver all souls and end their suffering.

**54.** “In this appearance, I will distribute throughout the world wealth that is difficult for even Brahmā to attain, and I will not consider who is fit or unfit.

**55.** “I will see how Kali devastates the souls, and I will reveal Nabadwīp Dhām.

**56.** “In the Dhām, I will break Kali’s poisonous fangs, perform kīrtan, and collect the souls.

**57.** “Kali will be subdued to the extent that My Name is chanted.”

**58.** Deciding this, Gaurahari revealed Nabadwīp by His own power at the beginning of Kali-yuga.

**59.** Gaurachandra held back Māyā’s darkness and revealed His eternal Pastimes in the land of Gauḍa.

**60–61.** I do not see anyone in the world during the Age of Kali as unfortunate, pitiable, and fallen as someone who does not serve such a merciful Lord and rejects such an inconceivable abode.

**62.** Therefore, brother! Give up all other desires and attachments, and devote yourself exclusively to Nabadwīp Dhām.

**63.** Bhakti Vinod, whose aspiration is the shade of Jāhnavā and Nitāi’s feet, reveals the glories of Nadia.

---

# Chapter 3. Instructions for Circumambulating the Dhām

**1.** All glory to the son of Śachī, the moon of Nabadwīp! All glory to the avadhūt Nityānanda Rāy!

**2.** All glory to the worshippable Śrī Advaita Prabhu! All glory to Gadādhar Paṇḍit and Śrīvās Paṇḍit!

**3.** All glory to Nabadwīp Dhām, the best of all abodes, the abode with which Gaurachandra appeared!

**4.** Devotees, listen! I will now describe everything within the thirty-two miles of Nabadwīp.

**5.** Sixteen rivers exist eternally within the thirty-two miles of Nabadwīp.

**6.** Four islands are on the east bank of the original Gaṅgā, and five are on the west bank.

**7.** These heavenly rivers flow throughout the islands and always beautify Nabadwīp Dhām.

**8.** Gaṅgā Devī’s original stream always flows through the centre of the Dhām; the other holy rivers flow in separate streams.

**9.** The beautiful Yamunā flows near the Gaṅgā. The Saraswatī, the carrier of knowledge, flows in another channel.

**10.** East of the Yamunā are the three long rivers: the Tāmraparṇī, Kṛtamālā, and Brahmaputra.

**11.** The Sarayū, Narmadā, Sindhu, Kāverī, Gomatī, and Godāvarī flow swiftly on the west side.

**12.** All these rivers cross each other and divide Nabadwīp into nine parts.

**13.** By the will of the Lord, these rivers sometimes go dry, and when the Lord wills it, they again fill with water.

**14.** By the will of the Lord, they sometimes submerge portions of land and then again reveal them.

**15.** The Dhām performs Pastimes eternally in this way and always appears before fortunate souls.

**16.** When intense desire arises in the heart of a devotee, all the islands and rivers in the Dhām reveal themselves.

**17.** Sometimes in dreams, sometimes in meditation, and sometimes in their vision, devotees see the Dhām through their devotion.

**18.** All the scriptures call the island at the confluence of the Gaṅgā and Yamunā Antardwīp.

**19.** Within Antardwīp is Māyāpur, where Lord Chaitanya appeared.

**20.** Devotees know Mahāvan in Goloka to be Māyāpur in Nabadwīp.

**21.** Śvetadwīp, Vaikuṇṭha, and Goloka Vṛndāvan are always present in Nabadwīp.

**22–23.** The seven great cities—Ayodhyā, Mathurā, Māyā, Kāśī, Kāñchī, Avantī, and Dvārakā—exist eternally in Nabadwīp in their respective places, by the arrangement of Gaurachandra.

**24.** The true form of the city Māyā, the gateway of the Gaṅgā, is Māyāpur, whose glories are stated repeatedly throughout the scriptures.

**25.** Those who once go to Māyāpur easily cross over material illusion.

**26.** If a soul wanders in Māyāpur, then the influence of Māyā goes away, and they do not take birth again.

**27.** North of Māyāpur is Sīmantadwīp. The sādhus and scriptures always explain the procedure for circumambulating the Dhām.

**28.** O wise devotees, visit Śrī Māyāpur in Antardwīp and then go to Sīmantadwīp.

**29.** Godrumadwīp is south of Māyāpur. Walk there and then joyfully go to Madhyadwīp.

**30.** Visit these four islands on the east bank of the Gaṅgā and then slowly cross the river.

**31.** Peacefully walk through Koladwīp and then see the beauty of Ṛtudwīp.

**32.** O best of the wise, thereafter, see the wonderful beauty of Jahnudwīp and then go to Modadrumadwīp.

**33.** O devotees, see Rudradwīp, cross the Gaṅgā again, and then visit Māyāpur once more.

**34.** There, slowly enter the abode of Śrī Jagannāth and Śachī to see the Lord.

**35.** Circumambulation is always done in this way and is a source of unlimited joy for the soul.

**36.** Everyone concurs that it is best to start on Mākarī Saptamī and end on Phālgunī Pūrṇimā.

**37–38.** Nitāi and Gaurāṅga bless the great souls who complete their circumambulation and visit Māyāpur on the birthday of the Lord. They make them qualified for devotion and give them the shade of Their feet.

**39.** I have briefly described circumambulating the Dhām. Now I will elaborate. Please listen.

**40.** Those who traverse the one hundred and sixty-eight miles of Śrī Gauḍa Maṇḍal quickly attain the wealth of divine love for Gaura.

**41.** Bhakti Vinod, whose aspiration is the shade of Jāhnavā and Nitāi’s feet, reveals the glories of Nadia.

---

# Chapter 4. Śrī Jīva Hears the Glories of the Dhām

**1.** All glory to the son of Śachī, the moon of Nabadwīp! All glory to the avadhūt Nityānanda Rāy!

**2.** All glory to the best of all abodes, Nabadwīp, where Śrī Chaitanya appeared!

**3.** Brother, souls attain in Nabadwīp in one day the result that others attain by residing in all the holy places.

**4.** O sādhus, listen as I discuss the scriptures and sing about circumambulating Nabadwīp.

**5.** The writing of the scriptures, the words of the devotees, and the order of the Lord are my life and wealth.

**6.** Having taken shelter of them, I will describe the procedure for circumambulating Nadia. Everyone listen.

**7.** When Śrī Jīva Goswāmī left home, he was eager at heart, calling out, “Nadia! Nadia!”

**8.** As he left Chandradwīp and walked down the road, his eyes filled with tears.

**9–10.** Again and again, he called out, “O Gaurāṅga! O Nityānanda! O Life of the soul! When will You mercifully appear before me? O Nabadwīp Dhām, best of all abodes! When will I see you?”

**11.** Jīva was young and had a beautiful figure. His extreme detachment was astonishing to see.

**12.** After walking for many days, always filled with divine love, Jīva arrived in Nabadwīp.

**13.** Seeing Nabadwīp from a distance, he bowed and fell almost unconscious.

**14.** Eventually he steadied his heart and entered Nabadwīp, the hair on his body standing on end.

**15.** He came to Bārakoṇā Ghāṭ and asked everyone, “Where is Nityānanda Prabhu? Please show me.”

**16.** Seeing Śrī Jīva’s ecstasy, a great soul immediately brought him to Nityānanda Prabhu.

**17.** Knowing that Śrī Jīva would come, Nityānanda Prabhu was delighted at heart and roaring with laughter.

**18.** He ordered His servants to bring Śrī Jīva, and many devotees went to call him.

**19.** Upon seeing the body of Jīva filled with divine ecstasy, Nityānanda Prabhu’s servants identified him.

**20.** Some of them, filled with intense divine love, went forward and conveyed the order of Nityānanda Prabhu to Jīva.

**21.** Hearing the Name of Nityānanda Prabhu, Jīva fell to the ground unconscious.

**22.** Immediately, he arose and said, “It is my great fortune that I, a fallen soul, have received the mercy of Nityānanda Prabhu.”

**23.** Jīva prostrated himself before all the devotees with a jubilant heart.

**24.** He said, “All the scriptures say that if you all are merciful to me, then I will attain the feet of Nityānanda Prabhu.”

**25.** Seeing Jīva’s fortune, some of the devotees joyously took dust from his feet.

**26.** They all joined together and took Jīva to Nityānanda Prabhu, where He was speaking about Kṛṣṇa, surrounded by devotees.

**27.** Seeing the divine form of Nityānanda Prabhu, extraordinary ecstasy arose in Jīva’s body.

**28.** Thinking, “What a wonderful form I have seen today!”, Jīva fell to the ground unconscious.

**29.** Moved by intense compassion, Nityānanda Prabhu raised Jīva to His feet.

**30.** Śrī Jīva Goswāmī quickly stood up, joined his palms, and began to speak to Nityānanda.

**31.** “You are Balarām, the original form and shelter of the world. I am just a soul. How can I understand Your glories?

**32.** “You are my Master, and I am Your eternal servant. The shade of Your feet is my one and only aspiration.

**33.** “Those whom you bless easily attain the feet of Śrī Chaitanya and float in the waters of divine love.

**34.** “Without Your grace, souls do not attain Śrī Gaura, even if they serve Him within their heart for one hundred births.

**35.** “If Gaura punishes someone, You protect them, and Gaura rejects whomever You punish.

**36.** “Therefore, O Lord, by my good fortune, I have taken shelter at Your lotus feet.

**37.** “O Lord, mercifully give Your consent that I may see Śrī Gaura and develop devotion to Him.

**38.** “When Śrī Gaurāṅga Rāy was in the village of Rāmakeli, my uncles took shelter at His feet.

**39.** “At that time, I was a child. With tearful eyes, I saw Śrī Gaura’s form, and still now that form constantly arises in my heart.

**40.** “Bowing at Śrī Gaurāṅga’s feet, I offered my obeisance. Touching His divine form, I felt great joy.

**41.** “At that time, Gaura said to me, ‘O J ī va, study the scriptures.

**42.** “‘Complete your study and then go to Nabadwīp. There, at the holy feet of Nityānanda, you will attain everything.’

**43–45.** “Holding this order on my head, I, a poor soul, have learned as much as possible. “I studied literature and other subjects in Chandradwīp, but I did not find an expert teacher of Vedānta. “The Lord ordered me to study Vedānta and prove that devotion to Kṛṣṇa is its purport.

**46.** “I have come to Your feet in Nabadwīp, and I will do whatever You order me.

**47.** “If You order me, I will go to the feet of the Lord in Puruṣottam Kṣetra and study Vedānta at the school of Sārvabhauma Bhattāchārya.”

**48.** Moved by Jīva’s sweet words, Nityānanda Rāy embraced Jīva and cried, unable to restrain Himself.

**49.** Nityānanda said, “O Jīva, listen to My confidential words. Rūpa and Sanātan know everything.

**50–51.** “The Lord ordered Me to tell you, ‘Do not go to Puruṣottam Kṣetra or stay here.’ “Everyone knows that you and the brothers Rūpa and Sanātan are exclusive servants of the Lord.

**52–53.** “The Lord’s order to you is that you go to Vārāṇasī, study Vedānta with Madhusūdan Vāchaspati, and then go straight to Vṛndāvan from there. Rūpa and Sanātan will bless you in Vṛndāvan.

**54.** “Serve the Divine Couple as a follower of Śrī Rūpa, and discuss Ved ā nta and other scriptures there.

**55.** “Preach that *Śrīmad Bhāgavatam* is the best of all the scriptures and the natural commentary on the *Vedānta-sūtra*.

**56.** “Śrī Gaurāṅga Hari mercifully explained the *Brahma-sūtra* to Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭāchārya on the basis of *Śrīmad Bhāgavatam*.

**57.** “Sārvabhauma very carefully taught this knowledge to Śrī Madhusūdan in Śrī Kṣetra Dhām.

**58.** “Go and see Madhusūdan Vāchaspati, who is in Vārāṇasī Dhām on the order of the Lord.

**59.** “Externally, he is a traditional Vedāntic scholar, and Śaṅkarite san nyāsīs study with him.

**60.** “In a gradual way, he mercifully teaches the sannyāsīs Gaurāṅga’s explanations of the sūtras.

**61.** “There is no need for a separate commentary on the *Brahma-sūtra* now. The *Bhāgavatam* explains the sūtras and is considered the sūtras’ natural commentary.

**62.** “In time, when there is a need for another commentary, the *Śrī Govinda-bhāṣya* will be revealed.

**63.** “Sārvabhauma’s relative, Gopīnāth Āchārya, heard the Lord’s commentary with Sārvabhauma.

**64.** “In time, by the will of the Lord, he will take birth as Baladev Vidyābhūṣaṇ and conquer Jaipur.

**65.** “There, he will compose a commentary spoken by Śrī Govinda, serve the feet of Gaurāṅga, and deliver the souls.

**66.** “Rūpa and Sanātan will explain all these confidential matters to you.”

**67.** Upon hearing the words of Nityānanda, Śrī Jīva Goswāmī rolled on the ground crying and lost consciousness.

**68.** Mercifully, the Lord placed His feet on Śrī Jīva’s head and empowered him.

**69.** Chanting, “All glory to Śrī Gaurāṅga! All glory to Nityānanda Rāy!”, Jīva then danced amidst the assembly of Vaiṣṇavas.

**70–71.** Śrīvās and the other great souls there, seeing the mercy of Nityānanda upon Jīva, danced, chanting, “Śrī Gaurāṅga! Nityānanda!”, and the loud sound of women cheering arose.

**72.** Eventually everyone stopped dancing, and Nityānanda sat down with Jīva.

**73.** Jīva stayed in the courtyard of Śrīvās, and in the evening he came to see Nityānanda Prabhu again.

**74.** Nityānanda Prabhu was sitting alone chanting the glories of Śrī Gaura. Jīva came and bowed at His feet.

**75.** Nityānanda Prabhu carefully sat Jīva nearby. Jīva joined his palms and humbly prayed to the Lord.

**76.** Jīva said, “O Lord, mercifully describe the glories of Nabadwīp Dhām for me.”

**77.** The Lord said, “O Jīva, I will describe the most confidential truths to you. Hold them in your heart.

**78.** “Do not reveal them anywhere now. They will be revealed after the manifest Pastimes of the Lord.

**79–80.** “Nabadwīp is the best of all the Lord’s abodes. Beyond Śrī Virajā, Brahmaloka, and Vaikuṇṭha is Śvetadwīp, Śrī Goloka, and lastly Gokula Vṛndāvan, Kṛṣṇa’s abode.

**81.** “That abode manifests in two ways: its rasa appears as either mādhurya or audārya.

**82.** “Aud ā rya is fully present in m ā dhurya, and m ā dhurya is fully present in audārya.

**83.** “Still, fortunate souls know that Vṛndāvan is where mādhurya is predominant.

**84.** “All the Vedas say that Nabadwīp Dhām is where audārya is always predominant.

**85.** “There is no difference between Vṛndāvan and Nabadwīp other than the difference made by their respective manifestations of rasa.

**86.** “This abode is eternally perfect, spiritual, and infinite. Materialists cannot even reach its edge.

**87.** “Only by the influence of the pleasure potency of the Lord do souls give up material nature and realise the true nature of the Dhām through eternal, perfect knowledge.

**88.** “The whole of Nabadwīp is a spiritual manifestation, and Śrī Gaurāṅga performs His Pastimes there.

**89.** “With fleshy eyes, people see a material form of the Dhām—Māyā keeps the eternal abode covered.

**90.** “In Nabadwīp, there is no illusory matter, space, or time. Souls undergo no suffering there.

**91.** “Souls bound by karma and bewildered by Māyā, however, enter Nabadwīp Dhām in a material way.

**92.** “When souls develop divine love by good fortune and the association of the sādhus, they then see the spiritual world.

**93.** “Souls easily and uninterruptedly see with their own eyes the supramundane space, time, light, and objects in the Dhām.

**94.** “Thus, I have described the glory of Nabadwīp. O Jīva, reflect on it with a pure heart.”

**95.** Bhakti Vinod, whose eternal aspiration is the feet of Nitāi and Jāhnavā, reveals the hidden glories of Nadia.

---

# Chapter 5. Śrī Māyāpur and Antardwīp

**1.** All glory to the son of Śachī, Śrī Chaitanya! All glory to the life of Jāhnavā, Śrī Nityānanda!

**2.** All glory to Nabadwīp Dhām, the best of all abodes, where Gaura descended during the Age of Kali!

**3.** Nityānanda Prabhu said, “Listen to My words! The thirty-two miles of Nabadwīp are nondifferent from Vṛndāvan.

**4.** “Within these thirty-two miles, there are nine islands, which form an eight-petalled lotus that floats on water.

**5.** “In the middle of the eight petals—the eight islands—is Antardwīp. At its centre is Māyāpur.

**6.** “The circular Yoga Pīṭh in Māyāpur is eternal. There, Śrī Chaitanya performs various eternal Pastimes.

**7–8.** “Its circumference is six miles, and its diameter two miles. “The Pañcha Tattva reside at the Yoga Pīṭh, whose greatness exceeds that of all other places.

**9.** “Very soon it will be hidden by the will of the Lord. The waters of the Gaṅgā will almost entirely conceal it.

**10.** “Someday, when the desire of the Lord becomes strong, the Yoga Pīṭh will be revealed and the Dhām will shine again.

**11.** “The eternal Dhām is never lost at any time—it only becomes hidden and then emerges again.

**12.** “My Lord is eternally present in Māyāpur on the eastern bank of the Gaṅgā.

**13.** “In the vision of common people, Viśvambhar became a sannyāsī, left Nabadwīp, and toured other places.

**14.** “In truth, however, My Gaurāṅga never leaves Nabadwīp Dhām or the village of Māyāpur.

**15.** “The devotees see His Pastimes here daily, and you too, Jīva, will see His dancing.

**16.** “Māyāpur shines within Antardwīp, where Brahmā saw Gaurāṅga.

**17.** “O Jīva, if you want to see everything, then circumambulate the Dhām, and you will be successful.”

**18.** Hearing the words of Nityānanda Prabhu, with tears in his eyes Jīva bowed at His feet.

**19.** “O Lord, if You are merciful to this poor soul, then please personally take me with You on a circumambulation of the Dhām.”

**20.** Hearing Jīva’s prayer, Nityānanda Rāy answered, “So be it.”

**21.** He said, “O Jīva, today see Māyāpur. Tomorrow we will tour extensively.”

**22.** Having said this, Nityānanda arose, and with a joyous heart Jīva rose after Him.

**23.** Nityānanda Rāy moved slowly, His body completely overwhelmed by divine love for Gaurāṅga.

**24.** Nityānanda Prabhu’s enchanting form swayed to and fro in ecstasy, and the ornaments all over His body shone.

**25.** The feet that Brahmā and Śiva cannot attain by meditation mercifully walked in front of Śrī Jīva.

**26.** Remaining behind, Jīva collected dust from their footprints, smeared it all over his body, and proceeded with great delight.

**27.** They entered the home of Jagannāth Miśra and spoke carefully at the holy feet of Mother Śachī.

**28.** “O Mother, listen! This is Jīva, a dear servant of Śrī Gaurāṅga. He is very intelligent and very fortunate.”

**29.** As Nityānanda Prabhu spoke, Jīva threw himself down like a tree uprooted by an intense storm.

**30.** He bowed at the feet of Śachī and rolled on the ground. Divine ecstasy surged through his body.

**31.** Śachī Devī mercifully blessed him, and that day he received prasād in her home.

**32.** Receiving the order of Śachī Devī, Viṣṇu Priyā cooked rice, various vegetables, and other things.

**33.** Śrī Vaṁśī Vadanānanda Prabhu then carefully offered the preparations to Śrī Gaurāṅga.

**34.** Īśān Ṭhākur then prepared a place and fed Nityānanda, with joy in his heart.

**35.** With motherly affection, Śachī Devī said to Nityānanda, “Eat, my child. Nityānanda, You are in the home of Your mother.

**36.** “Privately, I fed this to Gaurachandra. If You eat it, I will be very happy at heart.”

**37.** At the request of Mother Śachī, Nityānanda Rāy joyfully ate, and Jīva received His remnants.

**38.** Jīva said, “I am so fortunate to have received prasād at the home of Mahāprabhu in Māyāpur.”

**39.** After eating, Nityānanda Rāy took leave of Śachī Devī’s holy feet.

**40.** As He was leaving, He took Vaṁśī Vadanānanda with Him. Śrī Jīva bowed at the feet of Vaṁśī.

**41.** Nityānanda said to Jīva, “This is Vaṁśī Vadanānanda. The devotees know him to be Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s dear flute.

**42.** “By his mercy, souls become attracted to Kṛṣṇa and desire to join the Rāsa Dance.

**43.** “Jīva, look! Lord Chaitanya performed many Pastimes with us in this house.

**44.** “See here. This is the Temple of Jagannāth Miśra, where Gaura would earnestly worship Viṣṇu each day.

**45.** “In this house, the Lord would serve guests. See here His tulasī courtyard.

**46.** “When Śrī Gaurachandra, the maintainer of the devotees, was present at home, He would perform His father’s duties.

**47.** “Īśān Ṭhākur now performs these duties each day under the direction of Vaṁśī Ṭhākur.

**48.** “In this place, there was a great neem tree that disappeared upon being touched by the Lord.”

**49.** Nityānanda wept as He described all of this, as did Jīva and Vaṁśī.

**50.** Just then, Śrīvās arrived, and the four of them then left Jagannāth Miśra’s home.

**51.** Three hundred and twenty metres to the north, Nityānanda Prabhu joyfully showed Śrīvās Aṅgan to Jīva.

**52.** Jīva rolled on the ground in Śrīvās Aṅgan. Remembering the Pastimes of the Lord there, he was overwhelmed with divine love.

**53.** Jīva immediately had a vision: he saw Gaurāṅga dancing with His intimate devotees.

**54.** Jīva saw the Lord’s mahāsaṅkīrtan and wonderful dancing amidst all the devotees.

**55.** Advaita Prabhu, Nityānanda Rāy, Gadādhar, and Haridās were dancing and singing.

**56.** Seeing Śuklāmbar and hundreds of other devotees dancing, Jīva fainted in divine love.

**57–58.** When he regained consciousness and this vision disappeared, Jīva Goswāmī wept and lamented, “Why was I not born a little earlier? I was not fortunate enough to be part of such ecstatic kīrtan.

**59.** “The mercy of Nityānanda Prabhu is unlimited and infinite, and by its influence I have attained a moment of good fortune.

**60.** “I want to stay in Māyāpur forever. Here, the miseries of material existence will be completely dispelled.

**61.** “The order of the Lord, however, is greater than the desire of His servant, and my heart is palpitating at the thought of leaving Māyāpur.”

**62.** From there, Nityānanda took Jīva thirty-two metres north to the house of Advaita.

**63.** The Lord said, “O Jīva, see the house of Advaita. The devotees met here every day.

**64.** “Here, Advaita worshipped Kṛṣṇa and brought down our wealth, Śrī Gaurāṅga, with His cries.”

**65.** There, the four of them rolled on the ground. Then they walked sixteen metres east to the house of Gadādhar.

**66.** Thereafter, Nityānanda showed Jīva the houses of all the Lord’s associates throughout Māyāpur.

**67.** The four of them visited the homes of some brāhmaṇs and then joyfully walked to the bank of the Gaṅgā.

**68.** Along the outer boundary of Māyāpur on the bank of the Gaṅgā, Jīva saw the Temple of Vṛddha Śiva.

**69.** Nityānanda Prabhu said, “In Māyāpur, Vṛddha Śiva is the protector of the Dhām. His consort, Prauḍhā Māyā, resides with him eternally.

**70.** “When the Lord disappears, then, by His will, the Gaṅgā will swell.

**71.** “She will almost entirely cover Māyāpur with water for one hundred years, and then she will withdraw.

**72.** “At that time, only the land will emerge; no houses will remain. Māyāpur will stay uninhabited for a long time.

“Again, when the Lord strongly desires it, Māyāpur will be inhabited like this again.

**73.** “All these ghāṭs will appear on the banks of the Gaṅgā again, and the devotees will make Temples for the Lord.

**74.** “One wonderful Temple will manifest, and there daily worship of Gaurāṅga will flourish.

**75.** “By the will of the Lord, Prauḍhā Māyā and Vṛddha Śiva will return and perform their duties (of revealing and protecting the Dhām).”

**76.** Hearing this, Jīva joined his palms, questioned Nityānanda Prabhu, and then grasped the Lord’s feet.

**77.** “O Lord, You are the basis of the ultimate truth. You manifest Yourself as the Dhām and the Holy Name.

**78.** “Although You act according to the will of Mah ā prabhu, You are the Guru of all souls and the controller of all energies.

**79.** “The wise consider anyone who distinguishes between You and Gaurāṅga to be a sinner.

**80.** “You are the all-knowing Lord, who descends to perform Pastimes. A doubt has arisen in my heart.

**81.** “O Lord, please tell me, where will Vṛddha Śiva and Prauḍhā Māyā go when the Gaṅgā hides Māyāpur?”

**82.** Nityānanda Prabhu said, “Jīva, listen to My words and look at the land to the west of the Gaṅgā.

**83.** “See there the sand dune known as Pāraḍāṅgā. In that place, there is a village of brāhmaṇs.

**84.** “The wise know the bank of the Gaṅgā to its north as Chhinnaḍeṅgā.

**85.** “A village will be established on this bank. Vṛddha Śiva and Prauḍhā Māyā will remain there for some time.

**86.** “Who can describe the glories of that bank? The Rāsa Sthalī is there beside the Gaṅgā.

**87.** “It appears like a beach to fleshy eyes, but the Lord’s divine Pastimes take place in that eternal abode of gemstone.

**88.** “Māyāpur is Śrī Gokula Mahāvan, and Pāraḍāṅgā is considered Saṭṭīkāra.

**89.** “Śrī Rāsa Maṇḍal of Vṛndāvan is present there, and in the future there will be joyous singing at that place.

**90.** “O wise soul, know that Māyāpur and the Gaṅgā at Śrī Pulina are both part of Śrī Gaura’s Dhām.

**91.** “One who visits this abode of ten miles will see Māyāpur and Śrī Pulina.

**92.** “One who visits these ten miles with devotees on the full moon day in the month of Phālgun attains the eternal wealth.

**93.** “O Jīva, listen to My confidential words. Śrī Viṣṇu Priyā’s Deity of Śrī Gaurāṅga shines here.

**94.** “In the future, brāhmaṇs in the lineage of Jagannāth Miśra will bring this jewel-like Deity to Saṭṭīkāra Dhām.

**95.** “Four hundred years after the appearance day of Śrī Gaura, the worship of the Deity will be set to the highest standard.

**96.** “For now, keep all these matters secret and circumambulate the Dhām with a joyful heart.

**97.** “O best of the wise, see Śrī Gaurāṅga’s ghāṭ ten metres north of Vṛddha Śiva’s ghāṭ.

**98.** “On the pretext of childhood Pastimes, Gaurahari played here with the Gaṅgā and filled her heart with joy.

**99.** “Seeing the fortune of the Yamunā, Gaṅgā Devī had performed many austerities to become an associate of the Lord in His Pastimes.

**100.** “Kṛṣṇa mercifully appeared before her and said, ‘As Gaura, I will play in your waters.’

**101.** “The Lord of the three worlds performed this Pastime here. Upon seeing this place, fortunate souls feel great joy.

**102.** “The ghāṭ one hundred and sixty metres to the north is known by everyone as Mādhāi’s ghāṭ.

**103.** “Sixteen metres to its north is a beautiful ghāṭ that always attracts the minds of all the villagers.

**104.** “This extremely beautiful ghāṭ is known as Bārakoṇā Ghāṭ. Viśvakarmā constructed it on the order of the Lord.

**105.** “O Jīva, see the five Śiva Temples at this ghāṭ. Five effulgent liṅgams always reside here in these five holy places.

**106.** “These four ghāṭs beautify Māyāpur. Bathing at them removes all sorrow.

**107.** “To the east of Māyāpur is a place known as Antardwīp.

**108.** “Now, by the will of the Lord, it is uninhabited. It will remain this way for some time.

**109.** “Eventually, people will live here again, and the place will manifest as the glory of Nadia.

**110.** “O Jīva, today stay in Māyāpur. Tomorrow I will take you to Sīmantadwīp.”

**111.** Hearing this, Jīva said, “A doubt has arisen within me. Please hear it.

**112–113.** “When Gaṅgā Devī uncovers Māyāpur and it no longer remains hidden, by what signs will the devotees discover the hidden places? Please explain this.”

**114.** Hearing Jīva’s words, Nityānanda Rāy gave a nectarean answer.

**115.** “Listen, Jīva, when Gaṅgā Devī covers Māyāpur, one edge of it will remain manifest.

**116.** “Many uncivilised people will reside there, but the name of the place will remain Māyāpur.

**117–118.** “One thousand six hundred metres southwest, past this remaining place, a raised area always covered with grass will be visible. That place will be the home of Jagannāth Miśra.

**119.** “Sixteen metres from there is the Vṛddha Śiva Temple. According to these measurements, devotees will ascertain the lost places.

**120.** “A pond known as Śiva Ḍobā will be visible. Devotees will recognise that pond as the previous bank of the Gaṅgā.

**121.** “Know for certain that the devotees will reveal the lost places in this way by the will of the Lord.

**122.** “Four hundred years after the appearance of the Lord, the endeavour to reveal the lost places will be completed.”

**123.** Śrī Jīva said, “O Lord, now please tell me the true reason for Antardwīp’s name.”

**124.** Nityānanda Prabhu said, “At the end of Dvāpar-yuga, Brahmā performed austerities in this place, aspiring for the mercy of Gaura.

**125.** “Previously, using his illusion, he tried to trick Govinda by stealing the calves and cowherd boys.

**126.** “When he saw his illusion overpowered by Govinda, Brahmā became very sad, understanding his offence.

**127.** “He profusely praised Kṛṣṇa and prayed to Him. The Lord of Vṛndāvan then forgave Brahmā’s offence.

**128.** “Brahmā considered within his mind, ‘My mentality of being Brahmā is completely worthless.

**129.** “‘Because of my wrong mentality, I am devoid of divine love for Kṛṣṇa and deprived of relishing the rasa of Pastimes in Vraja.

**130.** “‘If I had been born as a cowherd boy, I would have easily served the Lord of the gopīs.

**131.** “‘I did not attain entrance into the rasa of those Pastimes, but now, with Śrī Gaurāṅga, I will not have any misconceptions.’

**132.** “Thinking in this way, Brahmā performed austerities and meditated for a long time in Antardwīp.

**133.** “Eventually, Gaurachandra mercifully came before Brahmā and spoke to him.

**134.** “‘O Brahmā, I am pleased by your austerities, and I have come to give you whatever you desire.’

**135.** “Opening his eyes and seeing Gaura Rāy, Brahmā fell to the ground unconscious.

**136.** “The Lord placed His foot on Brahmā’s head. Receiving divine knowledge, Brahmā praised the Lord.

**137.** “‘I am very lowly and fallen. Controlled by pride, I forgot Your feet and turned to material pleasures.

**138.** “‘The scriptures say that myself, Śiva, Indra, and the other gods are all servants under Your control.

**139.** “‘But we do not have the fortune of being Your pure servants, and thus Māyā covers us with her net of illusion.

**140.** “‘The first one hundred and fifty-five trillion years of my lifespan have passed. Now I am contemplating my final days.

**141.** “‘How will I pass the second one hundred and fifty-five trillion years of my lifespan? I know that if I remain averse to You, I will suffer greatly.

**142.** “‘My only prayer at Your feet is that I may become one of Your associates in Your manifest Pastimes.

**143.** “‘May I attain a birth in which my mentality of being Brahmā leaves me, and I live in Your company, chanting Your glories.’

**144.** “Hearing Brahmā’s prayer, the Supreme Lord Gaura said, ‘So be it’, and gave him the boon he desired.

**145.** “‘When My Pastimes manifest, you will be born in a home of uncivilised people.

**146.** “‘You will consider yourself lowly. You will be known as Haridās, and you will be free from pride.

**147.** “‘The Name will dance on the tip of your tongue three hundred thousand times a day, and you will see Me at the time of your passing away.

**148.** “‘As a result of your practice, at the end of the second one hundred trillion years of your lifespan, you will attain Nabadwīp Dhām and relish eternal rasa.

**149.** “‘O Brahmā, listen to My heartfelt words, but do not express them in the scriptures anywhere at any time.

**150.** “‘Taking the mood of a devotee, I will relish the rasa of devotion and reveal the most rare form of saṅkīrtan.

**151.** “‘I will completely madden all the devotees of My previous Avatārs with the rasa of Vraja.

**152.** “‘My heart is bound by the love of Śrī Rādhikā. I will take Her heart and halo, and appear.

**153.** “‘What sort of happiness does Rādhā feel by serving Me? Taking Her heart, I will relish that happiness.

**154.** “‘From today, you are My disciple, and as Haridās, you will serve Me eternally.’

**155.** “Saying this, Mahāprabhu disappeared, and Brahmā fell heavily to the ground unconscious.

**156.** “‘O Gaurāṅga! O Friend of the fallen! O affectionate Lord of the devotees! When will I reach Your lotus feet?’

**157.** “After crying and crying in this way for some time, Brahmā returned to his abode to perform his duties.”

**158.** This lowly, fallen, insignificant soul, who aspires only for the feet of Nitāi and Jāhnavā, chants the glories of Nadia.

---

# Chapter 6. Visiting Śrī Gaṅgā Nagar, Śrī Pṛthu Kuṇḍa, Śrī Sīmantadwīp, Śrī Viśrām Sthān, and Other Places

**1.** All glory to the son of Śachī, Śrī Chaitanya! All glory to the life of Jāhnavā, Nityānanda Prabhu!

**2.** All glory to the Lord of Sītā Devī, Advaita Āchārya! All glory to Gadādhar, Śrīvās, and all the Lord’s associates!

**3.** The next day in the morning, Nityānanda Prabhu left the Temple with Śrīvās and Śrī Jīva.

**4.** Rāmadās and other devotees accompanied them, and they chanted the Name of Gaura as they walked.

**5.** When the Lord came to the boundary of Antardwīp, He showed Śrī Gaṅgā Nagar to Jīva.

**6.** Nityānanda Prabhu said, “Listen Jīva, this is Gaṅgā Nagar. It was established by Mahārāj Bhagīrath, a forefather of Lord Rāmachandra.

**7.** “When the Gaṅgā, who is also known as Bhāgīrathī, came here, Mahārāj Bhagīrath went ahead of her blowing a conch.

**8.** “Arriving in Nabadwīp Dhām, the Gaṅgā stopped, and Mahārāj Bhagīrath saw that she would not proceed.

**9.** “Overwhelmed with fear, Mahārāj Bhagīrath retraced his course and came before the Gaṅgā.

**10.** “He stayed in Gaṅgā Nagar and began to perform austerities. Pleased by them, Gaṅgā personally appeared before him.

**11.** “Mahārāj Bhagīrath said, ‘O Mother, if you do not continue, my ancestors will never be delivered.’

**12.** “Gaṅgā said, ‘Listen, my child, O heroic Bhagīrath! Peacefully stay here for some time.

**13.** “‘We have come to Nabadwīp Dhām in the month of Māgh. At the end of Phālgun, I will deliver your ancestors.

**14.** “‘O Bhagīrath, I am the water that washes the feet of the Lord. Here in His abode, I will first fulfil my own desires.

**15.** “‘The full moon day in Phālgun is the birthday of the Lord, and I should perform a rite on that day.

**16.** “‘I will complete this rite, and then certainly I will go with you. Do not be afraid.’

**17.** “Mahārāj Bhagīrath resided in Gaṅgā Nagar until the full moon day in Phālgun.

**18–19.** “Anyone who stays in Gaṅgā Nagar on the full moon day in Phālgun, bathes in the Gaṅgā, fasts, and worships Śrī Gaurāṅga crosses over material existence, as do their ancestors.

**20.** “Along with one thousand of their ancestors, they attain Śrī Goloka, regardless of how or where they died.

**21.** “O Jīva, the glories of this place are unlimited. Śrī Chaitanya danced here many times.

**22.** “See there the homes of Gaṅgā Dās Paṇḍit and Sañjay Dās, which are always filled with joy.

**23.** “O best of the wise, hear now about the glories of the beautiful lake to the east.

**24.** “It has now become known as Ballāl Dīrghikā, but it was described in many other ways during Satya-yuga.

**25–26.** “When Mahārāj Pṛthu was cutting and levelling all the high and low places on the earth, he came to level this place, and an extremely bright light appeared in all directions.

**27.** “The workmen informed the king, and Mahārāj Pṛthu came and saw the light.

**28.** “Mahārāj Pṛthu, a Śaktyāveś-avatār, through meditation understood this place to be Nabadwīp.

**29.** “To keep the glories of this place hidden, he ordered, ‘Make a beautiful lake here.’

**30.** “The lake he made, known as Pṛthu Kuṇḍa, became famous all over Nabadwīp Dhām.

**31.** “How can I describe how much joy the villagers felt when they drank the pure water of this lake?

**32.** “Later, King Lakṣmaṇ Sen excavated, deepened, and enlarged the lake.

**33.** “Desiring to deliver his ancestors, he named the lake Ballāl Dīrghikā (after his father, Ballāl Sen).

**34.** “See there that large beautiful hill. There stood the house of Lakṣmaṇ Sen. It has since fallen into ruin.

**35.** “Various kings made all these ornaments at this extremely holy place to continuously accrue good fortune.

**36.** “Later, an uncivilised king desecrated this place, and thus devotees do not worship it.

**37.** “The land in this place is pure, but fearing interaction with the uncivilised people, devotees do not reside here.

**38.** “Offences were made against Deities in this place, and thus devotees left.”

**39.** Saying this, Nityānanda roared. He then came to Simuliyā Grām.

**40.** Seeing Simuliyā, Nityānanda Prabhu said to Jīva, “Know for certain that this is Sīmantadwīp.

**41.** “The sādhus call the island on the border of Nabadwīp and the southern bank of the Gaṅgā ‘Sīmanta’.

**42.** “In time, the Gaṅgā will completely cover this island, and only one pure place will remain.

**43.** “Hear now about how materialistic people will worship Pārvatī in that place, which is known as Simulī.

**44.** “Once, during Satya-yuga, the god of gods, Lord Śiva, was chanting the Name of Śrī Gaurāṅga and dancing.

**45.** “Pārvatī asked him, ‘Who is this golden Lord? Please tell me.

**46.** “‘Seeing your wonderful dancing and hearing the Name ‘Gaurāṅga’, my heart melts.

**47.** “‘I have understood that all the mantras and tantra I have heard about for so long are simply misery for the soul.

**48.** “‘So, please tell me about Lord Gaurāṅga. Serving Him, I will attain new life.’

**49.** “Hearing Pārvatī’s words and remembering Śrī Gaurāṅga, Lord Śiva spoke to Pārvatī.

**50.** “‘You are the primordial energy and an expansion of Śrī Rādhā. I will tell you the crown jewel of truths.

**51.** “‘This time, during Kali-yuga, Kṛṣṇa will take the heart of Rādhā and appear in Māyāpur in the womb of Śachī Devī.

**52.** “‘Maddened by His Pastimes of kīrtan, Lord Gorāmaṇi will distribute the jewel of divine love without judging its recipients.

**53.** “‘O goddess, shame on the life, enjoyment, and fortune of the souls who do not float in the waters of this flood of divine love!

**54.** “‘Remembering the promise of the Lord, I float in divine love. My heart has lost its composure, and thus I have left Kāśī.

**55.** “‘I will stay in a hut on the bank of the Gaṅgā in Māyāpur and worship Gaurāṅga.’

**56.** “Hearing Śiva’s words, beautiful Pārvatī quickly came to Sīmantadwīp.

**57.** “She constantly meditated on the form of Śrī Gaurāṅga. Chanting Gaura’s Name she floated in divine love, and her heart became unsteady.

**58.** “Eventually, Gaurachandra bestowed His mercy; He came with His associates and appeared to Pārvatī.

**59.** “His long form was the colour of molten gold, and His limbs and the wavy hair on His head appeared very beautiful.

**60.** “He wore a dhotī tucked in three places, and a wonderful flower garland swung on His neck.

**61.** “Stammering in divine love, Gaura Rāy said, ‘O Pārvatī! Why have you come here?’

**62.** “Pārvatī bowed at the feet of the Lord of the universe, and with an unsteady mind, explained her sadness.

**63.** “‘O Master! O Lord and Life of the universe! You are merciful to everyone, but cruel to me.

**64.** “‘O Saviour of the fallen, You have engaged me in imprisoning the souls who are averse to You.

**65.** “‘I perform this duty and manage the material world, but I am deprived of Your infinite divine love.

**66.** “‘People say that Māyā is absent wherever Kṛṣṇa is present. Thus, I have become completely estranged from You.

**67.** “‘O Lord, how will I see Your Pastimes? If You do not make a way for me, I will be hopeless.’

**68.** “Saying this, chaste Pārvatī Devī desperately rubbed Gaura’s foot-dust into the parting in her hair.

**69.** “Since then, this place has been known as Sīmantadwīp, though the ignorant call it Simuliyā.

**70.** “Moon-like Śrī Gaurāṅga was pleased and said, ‘O Pārvatī, listen to My words attentively.

**71.** “‘You are not separate from Me. You are My energy and the goddess of all. My consort, My singular energy, has two forms.

**72.** “‘As My personal energy, you are Rādhikā. As My external energy, Rādhā expands as you.

**73.** “‘Without you, My Pastimes are not complete. As Yogamāyā, you are certainly involved in My Pastimes.

**74.** “‘In Vraja, you are eternally present as Paurṇamāsī, and in Nabadwīp, you are present as Prauḍhā Māyā with Lord Śiva, the guardian of the Dhām.’

**75.** “Saying this, Śrī Gaurāṅga disappeared, and Pārvatī’s heart became overwhelmed with divine love.

**76.** “Out of love for Śrī Gaurāṅga, Pārvatī remains here as the goddess of Sīmantadwīp and in Māyāpur as Prauḍhā Māyā.”

**77.** Saying this, Nityānanda quickly entered Kāzī Nagar with Jīva.

**78.** Nityānanda Prabhu said, “O Jīva, listen to My words. Kāzī Nagar is Mathurā.

**79.** “Here, Śrī Gaurāṅga performed kīrtan, delivered the Kāzī, and gave him the jewel of divine love.

**80.** “He who was Kaṁsa during Kṛṣṇa’s Pastimes in Mathurā became known as Chā̐d Kāzī during Gaurāṅga’s Pastimes.

**81.** “The Lord thus called the Kāzī His uncle. Out of fear, the Kāzī took shelter at Gaurāṅga’s feet.

**82.** “When Gaurāṅga began performing kīrtan, the Kāzī broke a mṛdaṅga and made a disturbance on the order of Nawab Hussein Shah.

**83.** “Hussein Shah, the emperor of Gauḍa at that time, was previously Jarāsandha. He influenced the Kāzī, who was his relative.

**84.** “In the form of Nṛsiṁha, the Lord frightened the Kāzī, and like Kaṁsa, the Kāzī cringed in fear.

**85.** “The Lord then gave him divine love and made him a great devotee. Those who hear of the Kāzī’s deliverance are fortunate.

**86–87.** “See the difference between Vraja and Nabadwīp: in Vraja, those who offend Kṛṣṇa attain static liberation, but in Nabadwīp, offenders attain the jewel of divine love. Thus, the Pastimes of Gaura are supreme.

**88.** “Gaura’s Abode, Name, Form, and Qualities do not consider offences and are adept at delivering souls.

**89.** “If offences are present in the mind of a practitioner, Kṛṣṇa’s Name and abode deliver them after a long time.

**90.** “Gaura’s Name and abode, however, immediately give souls divine love; offences do not obstruct them.

**91.** “O Jīva, see there the samādhi of the Kāzī. Seeing it dispels sickness and sorrow.”

**92.** Saying this, Nityānanda, smouldering with divine love, quickly went to Śaṅkha Vaṇik Nagar.

**93.** Arriving there, He said to Śrī Jīva, “See there the wonderful sight of Śaraḍāṅgā.

**94.** “The name Śaraḍāṅgā is also very beautiful. Jagannāth resides there with the śabaras.

**95.** “Previously, when a demon named Raktabāhu was on a rampage, the Lord came here with the śabaras.

**96.** “This place is nondifferent from Śrī Puruṣottam Kṣetra; Jagannāth certainly resides here eternally.”

**97.** They then passed through Tantuvāya Grām and saw the cottage of Kholāvechā Śrīdhar.

**98.** Nityānanda Prabhu said, “Here, Śrī Gaurāṅga chanted, rested, and blessed the devotees.

**99.** “This place thus became known as Viśrām Sthān. You may rest here in Śrīdhar’s cottage.”

**100.** When Śrīdhar heard of Nityānanda Prabhu’s arrival, he came, prostrated himself, and worshipped the Lord.

**101.** He said, “O Lord, be most merciful to this servant and rest here. This is my request.”

**102.** Nityānanda Prabhu replied, “You are very fortunate: Lord Gaurāṅga has bestowed His mercy upon you.

**103.** “Today, we will rest here.” Hearing this, Śrīdhar was satisfied.

**104.** With great care, Śrīdhar collected suitable ingredients and engaged a Vaiṣṇava brāhmaṇ to cook.

**105.** Once Nitāi and Śrīvās had been served, Śrī Jīva joyfully took their prasād.

**106.** Śrīdhar laid Nityānanda on a bed, and then he and his family massaged the Lord’s feet.

**107.** In the afternoon, Śrīvās took Śrī Jīva and joyfully showed him Ṣaṣṭi Tīrtha.

**108–109.** Śrīvās said, “Listen, O pure-hearted Jīva! Previously, when the gods heard that Mahāprabhu would descend in Nabadwīp, Viśvakarmā came to Nadia.

**110–111.** “To prevent a scarcity of water along the paths where the Lord would perform saṅkīrtan, Viśvakarmā dug sixty large ponds in one night, the last of which he made in the village of the Kāzī.

**112.** “Śrīdhar’s banana grove is beautiful to see. See also the lake nearby.
