**113.** “Mahāprabhu sometimes played in the water there and took Śrīdhar’s bananas.

**114.** “Still today, Śrīdhar collects banana flowers and stems, and gives them to Mother Śachī with joy in his heart.

**115.** “Near here is a place called Mayāmāri. O Jīva, see that it still exists there today.

**116–117.** “Please hear a story from the Purāṇas: once when Baladev was on a pilgrimage, He came to Nabadwīp and rested. At that time, some brāhmaṇs told Him about a demon named Maya.

**118.** “Hearing of the havoc raised by Mayāsura, Baladev furiously confronted him in a field.

**119.** “The demon fought with Baladev ferociously, and finally Baladev killed him.

**120.** “Since then, this place has become known as Mayāmāri. Today, I have told you a very old story.

**121.** “This place is known as Tālavan in Vraja and always appears before the eyes of the fortunate souls.”

**122.** That night, they stayed there, and the next day they continued on, chanting, “Hari! Hari!”

**123.** This servant, whose aspiration is the shade of Nitāi and Jāhnavā’s feet, reveals the glories of Nadia.

---

# Chapter 7. Śrī Suvarṇa Bihār and Śrī Deva Pallī

**1.** All glory to the moon-like Śrī Chaitanya, Nityānanda Prabhu, Advaita Āchārya, Gadādhar Paṇḍit, Śrīvās Paṇḍit, all the devotees attached to the feet of the Lord, and the best of abodes, Śrī Nabadwīp!

**2.** Leaving Viśrām Sthān, with Śrī Jīva the party proceeded to the village known as Suvarṇa Bihār. There, Nityānanda Prabhu said, “O Jīva, this is a wonderful place. Like all of Nabadwīp, it is beyond material nature.

**3.** “Here, in Satya-yuga, there was a king known by all as Śrī Suvarṇa Sen. He ruled for a long time. Eventually he grew old, but he did not retire from his duties.

**4.** “His mind absorbed in mundane affairs, the king thought only of how to accumulate more wealth. As a result of some unknown fortune, Śrī Nārad came here, and the king worshipped him elaborately.

**5.** “Nārad blessed the King. He then met with him privately and instructed him in the Absolute Truth. Nārad said, ‘O King, your days pass in vain as you think only of wealth within your mind.

**6.** “‘Understand that material wealth is meaningless and that divine knowledge is true wealth. Once consider within your heart that your wife, sons, and friends do not belong to you. After death, no one belongs to anyone.

**7.** “‘When you die, everyone will set your body afloat down the river and then go back to their houses. So, why this false hope? Why this thirst for the water of sense objects, when no one actually belongs to anyone?

**8.** “‘You may say, “I enjoy life; I do not suffer. Therefore, I endeavour for wealth.” O King, such words are false. Life is temporary and does not last more than a hundred years.

**9.** “‘So, understand the essence: we must cross over māyā to the land where happiness does not produce sadness. Tell me, how will we reach that wonderful land where there is no lamentation, sadness, or fear?

**10.** “‘We cannot attain it by mere renunciation or knowledge. Brother! Through renunciation and knowledge, material bondage falls away, and souls enter oneness.

**11.** “‘There is no joy in oneness; we consider oneness total ruination. Oneness is utterly condemned. Understand oneness: by it, material things disappear, but nothing superior is attained.

**12.** “‘Thus, the wise do not accept enjoyment or liberation—they practise devotion to Kṛṣṇa. To be detached from mundanity and attached to Kṛṣṇa is the relationship, the process, and the goal.

**13.** “‘The soul is a servant of Kṛṣṇa, and without devotion, the soul is utterly ruined. The fruit of divine love for Kṛṣṇa grows on the tree of devotion; that fruit—the eternal wealth of the soul—is the goal. Enjoyment and liberation are both insignificant.

**14.** “‘Kṛṣṇa is the joyful, spiritual sun, Māyā is His shadow, and the souls are His atomic rays. If souls, governed by their marginal nature, touch Māyā, then Māyā binds them.

**15.** “‘The souls that turn away from Kṛṣṇa touch Māyā, and thus become bound in karma. They wander and die in Māyā’s prison. Neither action nor knowledge help them, and Māyā causes them to invite in their own suffering and ruin.

**16.** “‘Souls sometimes perform action, sometimes practise aṣṭāṅga-yoga, sometimes discuss theory of Brahma, and sometimes argue. Ultimately, none of these things help them, however, and they fail to appreciate the wealth of the self.

**17.** “‘After wandering and wandering in this way, when souls attain the association of devotees, they develop pure faith. By serving Kṛṣṇa in the association of the sādhus, souls free their hearts from impediments and attain pure constancy.

**18.** “‘Then, as they continue to serve, their constancy develops into attraction and, gradually, attachment to Kṛṣṇa. From attachment, feeling develops, and thereafter divine love. Pure devotion awakens in these stages.

**19.** “‘Hearing, chanting, remembering, serving, worshipping Kṛṣṇa, bowing, following, befriending, and surrendering—souls who engage in these nine practices in the association of devotees attain the wealth of divine love for Kṛṣṇa.

**20.** “‘O King, you are fortunate. You reside in Nabadwīp, and your fortune has arisen by residing in the Dhām. So, in the association of sādhus, develop faith, chant Kṛṣṇa’s Name and glories, and thus cause the sun of divine love to rise.

**21.** “‘The coming of Kali-yuga is glorious! Kṛṣṇa will manifest His Pastimes as Śrī Gaurāṅga with His associates here, and those who chant the Name of Gaura will receive the mercy of Kṛṣṇa and reside in Vraja.

**22.** “‘Those who serve Kṛṣṇa but do not chant the Name of Gaura attain Kṛṣṇa after a long time, but those who chant the Name of Gaura immediately attain Kṛṣṇa—offences do not remain in them.’

**23.** “As he spoke, Nārad Muni suddenly became overwhelmed. He began to dance, chanting, ‘Gaurahari!’, and as he did so, his vīṇā also chanted ‘Gaurahari!’ He mused, ‘When will the glorious Age of Kali arrive?’

**24.** “Having spoken to the king, Nārad left, and the king’s divine love arose. Chanting ‘Gaurāṅga’, the king danced and begged for divine love from the sādhus. His material desires disappeared.

**25.** “While asleep, the king had a vision of Gaura and Gadādhar with Their associates in his courtyard. Everyone was dancing, chanting ‘Hare Kṛṣṇa’, and embracing in the company of the golden Lord Gaura.

**26.** “When he awoke, the king became deeply dismayed and cried for Gaura. Then he heard a divine voice: ‘O King, when the Lord appears, you will be one of His associates.

**27.** “‘O virtuous King, you will be named Buddhimanta Khā̐n, and you will serve the holy feet of Gaurāṅga.’ Hearing this divine voice, the king became steady and then worshipped Gaurāṅga.”

**28.** When Nityānanda finished speaking, Śrīvās became immersed in the mood of Nārad and fainted. Śrī Jīva rolled on the ground, immersed in intense divine love and the intoxicating nectar of Gaura’s Name.

**29.** “Oh! Gaurāṅga Rāy! Gorāmaṇi! Will I see Your golden form here?” As Jīva spoke, everyone suddenly saw Śrī Gaura’s kīrtan with their own eyes.

**30.** Oh! The form of Gaurāṅga, which surpasses nectar, began to dance there, and Nityānanda Rāy chanted the glories of Gaurāṅga with Advaita and all the devotees.

**31.** Mṛdaṅgas and cymbals resonated, the saṅkīrtan resounded, and the previous Pastimes of the Lord manifested. I do not have the power to describe the joy that arose and remained there until midday.

**32.** Eventually, the party continued on, loudly singing songs about Gaurāṅga. They rested in the village of Deva Pallī as guests of the village Deity and then ate at midday.

**33.** In the evening, they toured the village, and then Nityānanda Prabhu said, “This is Deva Pallī. This Temple of Śrī Nṛsiṁhadev was established in Satya-yuga.

**34.** “After blessing Prahl ā d and killing Hira ṇ yaka ś ipu, the Lord rested here. Brahmā and the other gods made their own homes here and established a village.

**35.** “They resided on these hills beside the bank of the Mandākinī and worshipped Nṛsiṁha. The scriptures state that this abode in Nabadwīp, known as Śrī Nṛsiṁha Kṣetra, is very purifying.

**36.** “Sūrya’s hill and Brahmā’s hill were to the east of Nṛsiṁha. Now this place has since changed. See Gaṇeśa’s hill and Indra’s hill behind. This place was filled with many hills like these.

**37.** “The renowned Viśvakarmā constructed hundreds of stone homes for the gods. In time, they decayed, and the Mandākinī dried up. Only the hills remain.

**38.** “See here the countless broken stones. These are the ruins of the homes of the gods. Again in the future, a devotee-king will receive a drop of mercy from Nṛsiṁha.

**39.** “He will build a grand Temple, establish Nṛsiṁha there, and resume His worship. This place is within the thirty-two miles of Nabadwīp and is one of the boundaries of the route to circumambulate Nabadwīp Dhām.”

**40.** The humble Bhakti Vinod, whose wealth is the feet of Nitāi and Jāhnavā, chants the unending glories of Nabadwīp and thus leaves behind the net of māyā.

---

# Chapter 8. Śrī Harihara Kṣetra, Śrī Vārāṇasī, and Śrī Godruma

**1.** All glory to the son of Śachī! All glory to the Avadhūt!

**2.** All glory to Advaita Āchārya, the leader of the devotees! All glory to Gadādhar and all the devotees!

**3.** All glory to beautiful Nabadwīp Dhām! All glory to the Name of Gaura!

**4.** Nitāi and the devotees then departed, chanting “Hari! Hari!”

**5.** Swaying to and fro in ecstasy, Nitāi walked and stammered with divine love.

**6.** Tears streamed from His eyes. Chanting, “Gorā! Gorā!”, He became overwhelmed.

**7.** His ornaments and garlands sparkled, and His form illumined the ten directions.

**8.** Śrīvās danced with Jīva. Sometimes they cried, and sometimes they danced continuously.

**9.** All the other devotees danced as they walked.

**10.** Coming to the Alakānandā River, Nitāi spoke, floating in joy.

**11.** “Flowing west of Bilvapakṣa Grām, the Mandākinī comes and surrounds Nadia.

**12.** “Then she separates from the Alakānandā where we saw Suvarṇa Bihār.

**13.** “On the eastern shore of the Alakānandā, beside the Gaṇḍaka River, is Harihara Kṣetra.

**14.** “In time, a Deity will manifest there, and a beautiful forest will shine gloriously.

**15.** “See Kāśī to the west of the Alakānandā. There the followers of Śiva and Śakti worship the maidservant Liberation.

**16–17.** “This place is superior to Vārāṇasī because here Lord Śiva constantly chants, “Gaura! Gaura!”, dances holding his trident, and offers his followers devotion to Gaura.

**18–19.** “Here, devotees chant Gaurāṅga’s Name, dance, and kick away the liberation that sannyāsīs attain by residing in Kāśī and practising jñāna-yoga for a thousand years.

**20.** “Here, when souls pass away, Śiva chants the Name of Gaura into their ears and delivers them.

**21.** “This place is thus Mahāvārāṇasī. Souls have no fear of death here.”

**22.** Saying this, Nitāi danced and offered Jīva divine love for Gaurahari.

**23.** Lord Śiva then invisibly bowed at the feet of Nitāi.

**24.** Chanting the Name of Gaurāṅga, Śiva and Pārvatī fulfilled their desires.

**25.** Then the independent Lord, Nitāi, left with the devotees.

**26.** Arriving in Gādigāchhā Grām, Nityānanda came over and spoke, smiling.

**27.** “This island is called Godruma. Surabhi resides here eternally.

**28–29.** “When Indra, bewildered by Kṛṣṇa’s illusion, flooded Gokula with his powerful rains, Kṛṣṇa lifted Govardhan Hill and carefully protected Gokula.

**30.** “After Indra’s pride was crushed, Indra recognised K ṛṣṇ a.

**31.** “To clear his offence, he bowed and grasped the feet of Kṛṣṇa.

**32.** “Kṛṣṇa, the ocean of mercy, forgave Indra and gave him the boon of fearlessness.

**33.** “Still, Indra remained fearful. He then spoke to Surabhi.

**34.** “‘I could not understand the Pastimes of Kṛṣṇa, and I committed a very heavy offence.

**35.** “‘I have heard that during Kali-yuga, Kṛṣṇa will perform wonderful Pastimes in Nadia.

**36.** “‘At that time, I will become bewildered and be an offender again.

**37.** “‘O Surabhi, you know everything. Please advise me about this.’

**38.** “Surabhi said, ‘Let us go and serve Nimāi in Nabadwīp Dhām.’

**39.** “Indra and Surabhi moved here and worshipped Gaurāṅga.

**40.** “Worshipping Gaurāṅga is very easy, and the results of doing so are easily distributed.

**41.** “They cried, chanting the Name of Gaurāṅga, and immediately saw Him.

**42–43.** “How extraordinary is His beauty and form! They saw the figure of Gaurāṅga—the embodiment of benevolence, the well of rasa—smiling and stammering, choked with divine love.

**44.** “Smiling, My Lord said, ‘I know your desire.

**45.** “‘I will appear in Nadia soon, and you will see Me fully then.

**46.** “‘During those Pastimes, you will serve Me, and the net of māyā will no longer bind you.’

**47.** “Saying this, the Lord disappeared. Beautiful Surabhi remained there.

**48.** “She stayed near a banyan tree and constantly worshipped the feet of Gaura.

**49.** “This place thus became known as Godrumadwīp. The Lord fulfils the desires of the devotees here.

**50.** “Anyone who builds a hut and serves here easily becomes immersed in the feet of Gaura.

**51.** “There is a wonderful story about Mārkaṇḍeya Muni, who was present on this island during the destruction of the universe.

**52.** “Mārkaṇḍeya Muni attained a lifetime of seven kalpas, and during the destruction of the universe, he underwent great difficulty.

**53.** “Everything became submerged in water, and he searched for a place to stay.

**54.** “Floating and floating about, he thought, ‘Alas! Why have I taken this boon?’

**55.** “During the destruction, only the thirty-two miles of Nabadwīp Dhām arise and give shelter to the devotees.

**56.** “Floating in the waves of the water, the sage suddenly lost consciousness.

**57.** “Being very merciful to him, Surabhi carefully rescued the sage.

**58.** “Regaining consciousness, Mārkaṇḍeya Muni saw the wonders of Godrumadwīp.

**59.** “He saw an abode spanning two billion miles around, resplendent with rivers and streams.

**60.** “Numerous trees and vines were shining, and birds were chanting the glories of Śrī Gaura.

**61.** “He saw a banyan tree that was eight miles wide, and he saw Surabhi beneath it.

**62.** “Stricken with hunger, the sage spoke to Surabhi.

**63.** “‘O goddess! Please save my life. Rescue me by giving me some milk.’

**64.** “Surabhi mercifully fed the sage with her milk.

**65.** “Revitalised, Mārkaṇḍeya Muni spoke to Surabhi again.

**66.** “‘O goddess, you are my mother, and the world is absorbed in your illusion.

**67.** “‘Not understanding the consequences, I accepted a boon of living for seven kalpas without dying.

**68.** “‘During the destruction of the universe, there are numerous miseries and extreme distress. There is no happiness.

**69.** “‘O Mother, tell me, what should I do? How can I overcome this distress?’

**70.** “Surabhi said, ‘Serve the feet of Śrī Gaura.

**71.** “‘This abode of Nabadwīp is beyond material nature—it is never destroyed.

**72.** “‘To fleshy eyes, it is only thirty-two miles, but it is the supreme abode Vaikuṇṭha and eternally faultless.

**73.** “‘Here, time and space are supramundane, and no one knows of material illusion.

**74.** “‘See the great wonder of Nabadwīp! The chaste Virajā flows all around it.

**75.** “‘Every part of Nabadwīp measures two billion miles, and at its centre is the grand city of Māyāpur.

**76.** “‘The eight islands of Nabadwīp are petals, and Antardwīp is their whorl.

**77.** “‘All the holy places, gods, and sages reside here and worship Gaurāṅga.

**78.** “‘O Mārkaṇḍeya, understand your fortune and take shelter at the feet of Gaurāṅga.

**79.** “‘Take shelter of the true dharma, and keep desires for enjoyment and liberation far away.

**80.** “‘By taking shelter of Gaurāṅga and serving Him, you will attain divine love in madhura-rasa.

**81.** “When that divine love appears in your heart, it will set you afloat in the rasa of the arts within the Pastimes of the Lord.

**82.** “‘You will take shelter at the feet of Rādhā in Vraja, and your heart will be engaged in the service of the Divine Couple.

**83.** “‘Know that the joy of service is unparalleled, and knowledge of static liberation is useless.’

**84.** “Hearing the words of Surabhi, the sage immediately joined his palms and spoke.

**85.** “‘When I serve the feet of Śrī Gaura, what will become of my destiny?’

**86.** “Surabhi then spoke the ultimate conclusion: ‘There is no judgement in the service of Śrī Gaura.

**87.** “‘When you call the Name of Śrī Gaura, all your karma will be destroyed.

**88.** “‘No reactions will remain any longer, and your attachment to material existence will disappear.

**89.** “‘And not only your karma, but the consequences of your pursuing liberation will also be eradicated.

**90.** “‘You will become immersed in the ecstasy of Gaurāṅga, living on this island and serving Him.’

**91.** “Hearing this, Mārkaṇḍeya floated in joy. He chanted the Name of Gaura, cried, and sometimes laughed.

**92.** “Jīva, see here the wonderful place where Mārkaṇḍeya received life.”

**93.** Hearing the glories of Gaurāṅga from the mouth of Nitāi, Jīva floated in the greatest joy.

**94.** They spent the day in that place and then went to Madhyadwīp, chanting “Hari!”

**95–96.** Knowing Nitāi and Jāhnavā’s feet to be all-in-all, the fallen Bhakti Vinod holds the order of Nitāi on his head and describes the glories of Nadia.

---

# Chapter 9. Śrī Madhyadwīp and Śrī Naimiṣa

**1.** All glory to Gaurachandra, Nityānanda, Gadādhar, Śrīvās, all the devotees of the Lord! All glory to their home, the best of abodes, Śrī Nabadwīp!

**2.** Immersed in divine love and madly chanting about Gaurāṅga, Nityānanda departed with the devotees at the end of the night, distributing divine joy.

**3.** Coming into Madhyadwīp and laughing, Nityānanda Prabhu said, “This is Mājidā Grām. The seven sages resided here and worshipped the moon-like Śrī Gaura.

**4.** “During Satya-yuga, the sages became immersed in chanting Gaura’s glories and prayed to their father, Brahmā, for the eternal wealth of divine love for Gaurāṅga.

**5.** “Four-headed Brahmā felt great joy and said to his seven sons[^09-1], ‘Go to Nabadwīp and chant the glories of Gaura. You will easily attain divine love.

**6.** “‘The mercy of the Dhām is supreme. Those who attain it, attain the association of the sādhus. Serving in the association of the sādhus and becoming immersed in divine love for Kṛṣṇa is the ultimate joy.

**7.** “‘Those whose hearts develop attachment to Nabadwīp attain residency in Vraja. The supramundane Dhām and the Name of Gaurahari are the only desires of the sādhus.’

**8.** “Properly understanding the instructions of their father, the seven sages came here, chanted the Name of the Lord, and danced. They prayed for divine love for Gaurāṅga and loudly chanted His glories.

**9.** “They prayed, ‘O Gaurahari! Just once mercifully reveal Yourself to us. Practising various dharmas, we became offenders, but we have now accepted devotion to be all-in-all.’

**10.** “The sages performed austerities and served Gaurahari with firm devotion. They ate nothing, never went to sleep, and constantly chanted the Name of Gaura.

**11.** “Once, at midday, merciful Gaurāṅga, in the company of the Pañcha Tattva, revealed Himself to the sages. As bright as a hundred suns, His effulgence was alluring to the minds of yogīs.

**12.** “How extraordinary was His form! How beautiful and golden was His figure! He had a forest garland around His neck, and the glittering of His ornaments illumined the directions.

**13.** “His charming glance, His wavy hair, the spot of sandalwood paste on His forehead, His dhotī tucked in three places, His sacred thread—all were extremely beautiful and further beautified by His garland of jasmine flowers.

**14.** “Seeing His form and becoming enchanted, the sages prayed, ‘May we take shelter at Your feet. Please give us the wealth of devotion to Your feet.’

**15.** “Hearing their prayer, Gaurahari mercifully said, ‘O sages, listen! Leave behind all other desires and the chains of exploitation and renunciation, and discuss only Kṛṣṇa.

**16.** “‘My Pastimes will manifest in Nadia soon, and you all will then see My Pastime of chanting My Name.

**17.** “‘For now, keep this secret. Remember My words and worship Kṛṣṇa at the ghāṭ you made in Śrī Kumār Haṭṭa.’

**18.** “When Gaura disappeared, the seven sages went to Kumār Haṭṭa. See now the seven hills shining here.

**19.** “They are arranged just as the seven sages are in the sky. Residing here, the sages attained Gaurahari without following the rules and regulations of yoga practice.

**20.** “See the river to the south of here; this is the Gomatī. She is extremely pure. See also the Naimiṣa forest.

**21.** “In the previous manifestation of the universe, as Kali became very strong, Śaunaka and other sages joyfully heard about Lord Gaura from the holy mouth of Sūta Goswāmī.

**22.** “Anyone who recites the Purāṇas here during the month of Kārtik leaves behind all miseries, becomes immersed in the Pastimes of Gaura, and easily attains Vraja.

**23.** “Once, Śiva left Nandī, rode Brahmā’s swan here with his devotees, and heard the Purāṇas and songs about the glories of Gaura.

**24.** “Chanting and dancing, Śiva’s followers from Kāśī surrounded him, chanted the Name of Gaurahari, and and showered flowers.”

**25.** Hearing the words of Nitāi, Jīva became ecstatic. He lost his composure and rolled on the ground, relishing the glory of the Dhām.

**26.** The devotees spent the day there with Nitāichā̐d. The next day, they went to see Śrī Puṣkara.

**27.** As constant service to Jāhnavā and Nitāi arises in his heart, this soul lovingly sings of the sweetness of the devotees and the glories of Nadia.

[^09-1]: According to the *Viṣṇu-purāṇa* (3.1.32), the seven ṛṣis of the current manvantara are Atri, Bharadvāja, Gautama, Jamadagni, Kaśyapa, Vasiṣṭha, and Viśvāmitra. In previous eras, these and numerous other ṛṣis served in this capacity. Because the seven ṛṣis are described here as sons of Lord Brahmā, the seven ṛṣis who received Śrīman Mahāprabhu’s mercy may have been Aṅgirā, Atri, Kratu, Marīchi, Pulaha, Pulastya, and Vasiṣṭha, as this group is the only group of ṛṣis listed as the seven ṛṣis in the scriptures (in this case the *Bṛhat-saṁhitā*) in which all seven of the ṛṣis are sons of Brahmā.

---

# Chapter 10. Visiting Śrī Brāhmaṇa Puṣkara, Śrī Uchcha Haṭṭa, and Other Places

**1.** All glory to Gaurāṅga, Nityānanda, and Advaita! All glory to Gadādhar and Śrīvās Paṇḍit!

**2.** All glory to Nabadwīp, the abode of pure love and devotion! All glory to the Names of Gaura and Nityānanda!

**3.** O souls of Kali-yuga, listen! Abandon renunciation and exploitation, reject religion and irreligion, and serve Nitāi and Chaitanya!

**4.** They are oceans of mercy. Brother! They will freely give you the essence of Vraja’s joy.

**5.** At dawn, Nityānanda Rāy took Jīva and went out to tour the Dhām.

**6.** He said, “Jīva, look at this beautiful village. Everyone now calls it Brāhmaṇpur.

**7.** “All the scriptures call it Brāhmaṇ Puṣkara. A very confidential Pastime took place here.

**8.** “During Satya-yuga, a brāhmaṇ named Diva Dās left home and visited all the holy places.

**9.** “He felt the greatest joy at Puṣkara Tīrtha. Still, he travelled, and eventually he came to Nabadwīp.

**10.** “Here, during the night, he had a dream in which he was told, ‘O brāhmaṇ, reside here and you will attain eternal wealth.’

**11.** “Diva Dās built a cottage and resided here into his old age.

**12.** “In his old age, the great brāhmaṇ was unable to travel, but he desired to see Puṣkara.

**13.** “Unable to travel, the brāhmaṇ cried, ‘I will never see Puṣkara again.’

**14.** “The Lord of Puṣkara felt compassion and revealed himself to Diva Dās in the form of a brāhmaṇ.

**15.** “He said to Diva Dās, ‘O brāhmaṇ, do not cry. There is a beautiful pond in front of you.

**16.** “‘Bathe in this pond once, and you will see Puṣkara Tīrtha.’

**17.** “Hearing this, the great brāhmaṇ bathed in the pond. Attaining divine eyes, he saw Puṣkara before him.

**18.** “Crying, the brāhmaṇ said to Puṣkara, ‘You have taken great trouble for me.’

**19.** “Puṣkara said, ‘Listen, O fortunate brāhmaṇ! I have not come from far away. I live here.

**20.** “‘Nabadwīp Dhām contains all holy places; all holy places reside here and serve Nabadwīp.

**21.** “‘Although a form of mine exists in the west, I myself live here eternally.

**22.** “‘Souls attain here the result attained by bathing a hundred times there.

**23.** “‘Thus, anyone who leaves Nabadwīp desiring to visit other holy places is a foolish, unfortunate soul.

**24.** “‘When souls attain the result of visiting all other holy places, then they can reside in Nabadwīp.

**25.** “‘See there the raised area that resembles a market. Kurukṣetra and Brahmāvarta exist there.

**26.** “‘With the Saraswatī and Dṛṣadvatī Rivers on its two sides, it shines brightly and bestows good fortune.

**27.** “‘O brāhmaṇ, I will tell you a secret. Very soon there will be great joy here.

**28.** “‘Gaurāṅgasundar will appear in the home of Śachī in Māyāpur and broadly distribute divine love.

**29.** “‘In all these places, the Lord will dance in the ecstasy of saṅkīrtan with the devotees and become mad.

**30.** “‘The Lord will perform kīrtan with all the devotees of all His previous Avatārs.

**31.** “‘He will inundate the entire world with a flood of divine love. Everyone except the speculators will attain intense divine love.

**32.** “‘O Diva Dās, souls who are faithful to the Dhām and reside here will reach the feet of Gaura.

**33.** “Unfortunate souls worship Kṛṣṇa for billions of years but do not attain attraction to His Name.

**34.** “‘If souls worship Gaurāṅga, their wickedness goes away, and they quickly attain Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa in Vraja Dhām.

**35.** “‘They attain their spiritual body and the shelter of the sakhīs, and serve the Divine Couple in their own grove.

**36.** “‘O brāhmaṇ, stay and serve here. You will see Śrī Gaurāṅga with His associates.’

**37.** “Saying this, the Lord of Puṣkara left. The brāhmaṇ then heard a voice come from the sky, ‘Glory to the Age of Kali!

**38.** “‘O brāhmaṇ, you will be born again at that time and swim in the divine love of Śrī Gaura’s kīrtan.’

**39.** “Hearing this, Diva Dās became fearless. He resided on the bank of the pond and performed worship.”

**40.** After telling Śrī Jīva these ancient stories, Nityānanda entered Uchcha Haṭṭa, Kurukṣetra.

**41.** Nityānanda said, “All the gods came here with Kurukṣetra Tīrtha.

**42.** “All the holy places in Brahmāvarta and Kurukṣetra came and resided here.

**43.** “Pṛthūdaka and the other holy places resided here and served Nabadwīp without difficulty.”

**44.** By staying here for one night, souls attain the full result of residing in Kurukṣetra for a hundred years.

**45.** Nityānanda Prabhu continued, “The gods resided here, established a market, and discussed Gaurāṅga.

**46.** “The name of this place thus became Haṭṭaḍāṅgā. By seeing this place, souls enter an ocean of divine love.

**47.** “O Jīva, see here one of the boundaries of Nadia. Let us now cross the Gaṅgā.”

**48.** Crossing the Gaṅgā, Nityānanda Prabhu arrived in Koladwīp at midday.

**49.** On the way to Kuliyāpāhāḍapur, Nitāichā̐d spoke to Śrī Jīva.

**50.** “The way we have come, crossing the Gaṅgā, is the proper way to circumambulate the Dhām.

**51–52.** “One evening, while performing saṅkīrtan with fourteen mṛdaṅgas, Śrī Chaitanya left Māyāpur with His associates and came to purify the Kāzī.

**53.** “That night, like a night of Brahmā, did not end quickly. Mahāprabhu toured His abode in this way.

**54.** “Thereafter, My Lord toured performing saṅkīrtan on every Ekādaśī.

**55.** “Sometimes He toured the ten miles of Antardwīp, and sometimes, as He desired, He toured for sixteen miles.

**56.** “From His home, He would leave Bārakoṇā Ghāt and go around Ballāl Dīrghikā to Śrīdhar’s house.

**57.** “From there, He would tour around the boundary of Antardwīp and then return. In this way, He would easily circumambulate for ten miles.

**58.** “Other times, passing Simuliyā, He would go to the house of the Kāzī, speak with Śrīdhar, and then come to Gādigāchhā.

**59.** “From Mājidā, He would cross the Gaṅgā and tour Pāraḍāṅgā and Chhināḍāṅgā along the broad riverbank.

**60.** “Then He would cross the Gaṅgā again and return to His home, thus touring for sixteen miles.

**61.** “A complete circumambulation is the full thirty-two miles. The Lord is pleased if someone completes this.

**62.** “I am taking you on this complete circumambulation. There is no circumambulation equal to it.

**63.** “During this circumambulation, you will see all thirty-two miles of the Dhām and the twelve forests of Vṛndāvan.

**64.** “This circumambulation is completed over the course of nine nights; the scriptures call it the ‘nava-rātra parikramā’.

**65.** “Devotees perform the ten-mile circumambulation in one day and complete the sixteen-mile circumambulation over the course of three nights.

**66.** “One night is in Māyāpur, the second is in Godrumadwīp, and the third night is in Śrī Pulina. Devotees tour in this way.”

**67.** Hearing about the process of circumambulation, Śrī Jīva Goswāmī became overwhelmed with divine love for some time.

**68.** This poor, fallen soul, whose aspiration is the shade of Nitāi and Jāhnavā’s feet, describes the glories of Nadia.

---

# Chapter 11. Śrī Śrī Koladwīp, Śrī Samudragaḍ, Śrī Champā Haṭṭa, and Śrī Jayadev

**1.** All glory to Śrī Chaitanya, Nityānanda, Advaita, Śrīvās, and all the devotees of the Lord!

**2.** All glory to the land of Gauḍa, the best of all lands, where Śrī Chaitanya descended with His Name!

**3.** Nityānanda Prabhu said, “Everyone, listen! Here, the Gaṅgā convenes with five other rivers.

**4.** “The Gaṅgā flows here with the Mandākinī and the Alakānandā. The Saraswatī is also hidden here.

**5.** “The Yamunā comes from the west with the Bhogavatī, and the swift Mānasa Gaṅgā also flows within them.

**6.** “Considering this place Mahāprayāg, sages have performed billions of sacrifices here with Lord Brahmā.

**7.** “The glories of this place of Brahmā’s sacrifices are unlimited. If a soul bathes here, they do not take birth again.

**8.** “Who can describe the glories of this place? It is not a holy place that is now simply a dry river.

**9.** “All souls who give up their life in the water, on the earth, or in the air here attain Śrī Goloka Vṛndāvan.

**10.** “This place is known as Kuliyāpāhāḍa. It is a raised land on the bank of the Gaṅgā, resembling a mountain.

**11.** “It is called Koladwīp in the scriptures. Everyone, listen to a story from Satya-yuga.

**12.** “There was a brāhmaṇ boy named Vāsudev. He regularly worshipped Varāhadev.

**13.** “He worshipped a Deity of Śrī Varāha and constantly prayed to Him.

**14.** “‘O Lord! Mercifully reveal Yourself to me so that my eyes and life may be fulfilled.’

**15.** “Praying in this way, the brāhmaṇ would roll on the ground and cry, ‘O Lord! If You do not reveal Yourself, my life is worthless.’

**16.** “Eventually, Śrī Varāha mercifully revealed Himself to Vāsudev in the form of a boar.

**17.** “His body was ornamented with various jewels and ornaments, and His feet, neck, nose, mouth, and eyes were enchanting.

**18.** “His body was as tall as a mountain. Seeing Him, the brāhmaṇ again and again felt himself to be fortunate.

**19.** “He fell to the ground, bowed at the feet of the Lord, cried fervently, and rolled on the ground.

**20.** “Seeing the devotion of the brāhmaṇ, Varāha spoke sweet words to Vāsudev.

**21.** “‘O Vāsudev! You are My devotee, and I am very pleased with your worship.

**22.** “‘When Kali-yuga arrives, I will perform Pastimes in Nabadwīp. Listen to My conclusive words.

**23.** “‘There is no abode like Nabadwīp in the three worlds; all of My most beloved abodes are hidden within it.

**24.** “‘Brahmāvarta and all other holy places that exist are present here. This is the conclusion of the scriptures.

**25–26.** “‘The holy place in which I appeared during Brahmā’s sacrifice and killed Hiraṇyākṣa, ripping him with My tusks, exists here where I have now appeared.

**27.** “‘All holy places exist here and serve Nabadwīp. By residing in Nabadwīp, you reside in all the holy places.

**28.** “‘You are fortunate: you have served Me in Nabadwīp. You will take birth here when I appear as Śrī Gaurāṅga.

**29.** “‘Easily, you will see His grand saṅkīrtan and extraordinary form.’

**30.** “Saying this, Śrī Varāha disappeared. A divine voice then arose to guide the brāhmaṇ.

**31.** “Worshippable Vāsudev was a great scholar. He studied all the scriptures and understood everything clearly.

**32.** “‘At the beginning of Kali-yuga during the reign of Vaivasvata Manu, the Pastimes of Śrī Gaurāṅga will manifest in Nadia.

**33.** “‘The sages kept this truth hidden but also hinted at it in the scriptures. The wise understand all of this.

**34.** “‘When the Lord appears, His Pastimes will be revealed, but for now they should be kept hidden.’ In this way, Vāsudev understood the purport of the scriptures.

**35.** “With great joy, the brāhmaṇ performed saṅkīrtan, chanting the Name of Gaura constantly within his heart.

**36–37.** “Having seen the mountain-like form of Varāhadev, the wise Vāsudev decided that this place would be named Koladwīp Parvat. Since then, it has been known as Parvat.

**38.** “O Jīva! Devotees know this place to be Govardhan Hill of Vṛndāvan, which is filled with the Lord’s eternal Pastimes.

**39.** “See Śrī Bahulāvan to the north. Its effulgence shines in all directions.

**40.** “O Jīva, the twelve forests are not arranged here the way they are in Vṛndāvan.

**41.** “By the will of the Lord, their order is reversed here. The whimsical Lord knows the reason for this.

**42.** “See the way they are arranged here. This extraordinary reversal increases everyone’s divine love.”

**43.** Nityānanda Prabhu went ahead and then said, “See here Samudragaḍ.

**44.** “O best of the wise, see here both Dvārakā Purī and Śrī Gaṅgā Sāgar.

**45.** “Śrī Samudra Sen was a king here. He was a great devotee of Kṛṣṇa—he knew nothing but Kṛṣṇa.

**46.** “When Bhīmasen came here with his army to conquer Bengal, he surrounded Samudragaḍ.

**47.** “The king knew Kṛṣṇa was the Pāṇḍavas’ only shelter, and that if the Pāṇḍavas faced danger, Kṛṣṇa would come.

**48.** “‘If I can cause Bhīma to show that he is afraid, Kṛṣṇa will feel compassion in response to his cries of distress.

**49.** “‘Then Kṛṣṇa will compassionately come to this servant’s land, and I will easily see His dark form with my eyes.’

**50.** “Thinking thus, the king prepared his army. Taking his elephants, horses, and foot soldiers, he went to battle.

**51.** “Remembering Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the king fired arrows. Overwhelmed by the arrows, Bhīma became extremely afraid.

**52.** “Seeing that he was in danger, Bhīma called out to Kṛṣṇa within his mind, ‘O Lord! Protect Bhīma with Your holy feet.

**53.** “‘I cannot fight with Samudra Sen, but I also cannot tolerate the great shame of retreating.

**54.** “‘O Kṛṣṇa! You are the Lord of the Pāṇḍavas. O merciful Lord! If we are defeated, it will be a great shame.’

**55.** “Hearing Bhīma’s sorrowful cry, Kṛṣṇa mercifully appeared on the battlefield.

**56.** “An extraordinary event then took place: Śrī Samudra Sen saw the Lord’s form, but no one else did.

**57.** “The Lord’s adolescent form was the colour of a fresh rain cloud, a forest garland swung on His neck, and His crown shone.

**58.** “He had extremely beautiful ornaments on all His limbs, and His extraordinary body was clothed in yellow garments.

**59.** “Seeing Kṛṣṇa’s form, the king fainted out of divine love. He then steadied himself and prayed to the Lord.

**60.** “‘O Kṛṣṇa, You are the Lord of the universe and the saviour of the fallen. Having seen this fallen soul, You have come here.

**61.** “‘The souls of this world glorify Your Pastimes, and hearing that, I desired to see You.

**62–63.** “‘My vow, however, O merciful Lord, was that You would appear in Nabadwīp: “I will see Your enchanting form here so that I do not have to leave Nabadwīp.”

**64.** “‘O merciful Lord, You have upheld my vow and appeared as Kṛṣṇa in Nabadwīp.

**65.** “‘Still, my very confidential desire is that You will appear before my eyes as Gaurāṅga.’

**66.** “The king suddenly saw before him the incomparably sweet forms and Pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

**67.** “Kṛṣṇa was tending cows and playing with His friends in Śrī Kumudavan in the afternoon.

**68.** “Within a moment, these Pastimes disappeared, and the king saw the form of Śrī Gaurāṅga, filling his eyes.

**69.** “Immersed in grand saṅkīrtan, the Lord was dancing and chanting with His devotees.

**70.** “The Lord’s extremely beautiful golden lustre made the eyes of the king quiver and his heart palpitate intensely.

**71.** “Seeing the form of the Lord, the king considered himself fortunate. He then offered prayers at the Lord’s feet.

**72.** “Eventually, everything disappeared. The king became thoughtful and began to cry.

**73.** “Bhīmasen did not see this miracle and thought that the king had suddenly become afraid to fight.

**74.** “Bhīmasen showed his immense strength, and the king happily submitted his tribute.

**75.** “Bhīmasen accepted the tribute and went elsewhere. Still now, the whole world sings of Bhīma as the conqueror of all directions.

**76.** “This place, Samudragaḍ, is on the boundary of Nabadwīp. Even Brahmā does not know the glories of this place.

**77.** “The ocean came to the shelter of the Gaṅgā here and, with the mood of a devotee, served the feet of the Lord.

**78.** “The Gaṅgā said, ‘O Ocean, very soon my Lord will stay in a forest on your bank.’

**79.** “The ocean said, ‘O goddess, listen to my words! The son of Śachī never leaves Nabadwīp.

**80.** “‘Although He will stay on my bank for some time, He will remain invisibly in Nadia.

**81.** “‘The Vedas sing of the manifest and unmanifest Pastimes of the Lord here in His eternal abode of Nabadwīp.

**82.** “‘O beautiful goddess! I will stay here under your shelter and serve Śrī Gaurāṅga in Nabadwīp.’

**83.** “Saying this, the ocean stayed in Nabadwīp, meditating on the eternal Pastimes of Gaurāṅga.”

**84.** Nityānanda Prabhu then came to Champā Haṭṭa Grām and rested there in the home of Vāṇīnāth.

**85.** In the afternoon, Nityānanda toured through Champā Haṭṭa and said, “O Jīva, listen!

**86.** “Previously, there was a beautiful champak forest here that was part of Khadiravan.

**87.** “Daily, Champakalatā Sakhī would pick champak flowers here, string garlands, and serve Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

**88.** “When Kali-yuga started, garland-makers eagerly began to take the champak flowers from the forest.

**89.** “They established a market and resided here. They would take the flowers and sell them, and the villagers would buy them.

**90.** “As a result, this beautiful place was named Śrī Champaka Haṭṭa, and people began to call it Chā̐pāhāṭi.

**91.** “When Lakṣmaṇ Sen was the king of Nadia, Jayadev Goswāmī was one of His subjects in Nabadwīp.

**92.** “The wise Jayadev built a cottage on the bank of Ballāl Dīrghikā and resided there with his wife Padmāvatī.

**93.** “While there, he wrote the *Daśa-avatāra-stotram*, and this poem once came to the hand of Lakṣmaṇ Sen.

**94.** “The king read the poem with great joy and then asked, ‘Who wrote this?’

**95.** “Govardhan Āchārya said to the king, ‘The great poet Jayadev wrote it.’

**96.** “The king asked, ‘Where is this poet Jayadev?’ Govardhan replied, ‘He lives in Nabadwīp.’

**97.** “Hearing this, the king secretly searched for Jayadev’s home and then came there in the evening.

**98.** “The king entered the cottage in the dress of a Vaiṣṇava. He bowed to Jayadev and sat on one side.

**99.** “Jayadev understood that this man was the king and that he had come to him as a beggar in the dress of a Vaiṣṇava.

**100.** “Within a short time, the king introduced himself and requested Jayadev to go to his palace.

**101.** “The wise Jayadev was extremely detached, and he did not agree to go to the home of any materialist.

**102.** “Jayadev, the great devotee of Kṛṣṇa, then said, ‘I will leave your province and go elsewhere.

**103.** “‘Association with materialists never results in good fortune, so I will cross the Gaṅgā and go to Nīlāchal.’

**104.** “The king said, ‘O master, listen to my words. Please do not ever leave Nabadwīp.

**105.** “‘Please be merciful to me and act in such a way that your words will come true and my desire will also be fulfilled.

**106.** “‘On the other side of the Gaṅgā, there is a beautiful place known as Champā Haṭṭa. Please stay there for a couple of years.

**107.** “‘I will not go there according to my own desire; I will come to see your feet only when you desire it.’

**108.** “Hearing the words of the king, the great poet immediately replied in agreement.

**109.** “‘Although you are a materialist and this kingdom is yours, you are a devotee of Kṛṣṇa and you have no material attachment.

**110.** “‘I called you a materialist to test you, but you heard this and tolerated it.

**111.** “‘I have now understood that you are a devotee of Kṛṣṇa. You engage in material affairs but remain detached.

**112.** “‘I will stay in Champaka Haṭṭa for some time. In secret, leaving your opulence behind, you may come to see me.’

**113.** “Through one of his ministers, the king happily had a house constructed in Champaka Haṭṭa.

**114.** “The poet Jayadev stayed there for some time and served Śrī Kṛṣṇa according to the path of divine love.

**115.** “Padmāvatī Devī would bring loads of champak flowers, and Jayadev worshipped Kṛṣṇa, the son of Nanda, with them.

**116.** “Jayadev offered worship with intense love, and eventually he saw Śrī Kṛṣṇa appear before him in a form the colour of a champak flower.

**117.** “The Lord’s beautiful, golden lustre was completely enchanting, and His extremely beautiful face belittled millions of moons.

**118.** “He had wavy hair, long arms, and a beautiful flower garland around His neck. His body illumined the thatched hut of Jayadev.

**119.** “Seeing the form of Gaurāṅga, Jayadev, the best of poets, fainted in divine love as tears streamed from his eyes.

**120.** “Seeing the Lord’s form, Padmāvatī Devī fainted and fell to the ground.

**121.** “The Lord raised them both with His lotus hands and then mercifully spoke nectarean words.

**122.** “‘You are both My highly exalted devotees, and I desired to reveal Myself to you.

**123.** “‘Very soon I will take birth from the womb of Śachī Devī in Nadia.

**124.** “‘With all the devotees of all My previous Avatārs, I will distribute the wealth of divine love through Śrī Kṛṣṇa-kīrtan.

**125.** “‘At the age of twenty-four, I will take sannyās and then reside in Nīlāchal.

**126.** “‘There, immersed in intense divine love, I will deeply relish your *Śrī Gīta-govinda* with My devotees.

**127.** “‘I am telling you the truth: your *Gīta-govinda* is extremely dear to Me.

**128.** “‘Nabadwīp Dhām is spiritual and supreme; I promise you that you will come here after leaving your body.

**129.** “‘For now, you two will go to Nīlāchal. Serve Jagannāth there, and you will attain the fruit of divine love.’

**130.** “Saying this, Gaurachandra disappeared. In separation from the Lord, Jayadev and Padmāvatī fainted.

**131.** “Regaining consciousness, they began to cry profusely. Crying and crying, they offered prayers.

**132.** “‘Alas! What a form we have seen! How will we now live in separation from Him?

**133.** “‘Why did the Lord order us to leave Nadia? We understand that we must have made some offence here.

**134.** “‘Nabadwīp Dhām is spiritual and supreme, and our hearts are dismayed at the thought of leaving.

**135.** “‘It would be better to become an animal or bird and remain here forever, meditating on the Dhām.

**136.** “‘We can give up our lives, but we cannot give up the Dhām—our attachment is so intense.

**137.** “‘O Lord! Śrī Gaurāṅga! Be merciful and keep us here at Your holy feet.’

**138.** “While speaking in this way, they cried aloud, and then they heard a divine voice.

**139.** “‘Go to Nīlāchal, and do not be sad or unsteady at heart. I will tell you two things.

**140.** “‘Some time ago, you both desired to stay in Nīlāchal for a few days.

**141.** “‘Jagannāth has fulfilled this desire of yours, and He wants to see you.

**142.** “‘You two will please Jagannāth, again leave your bodies, and then reside in Nabadwīp eternally.’

**143.** “Upon hearing the divine voice, Jayadev and Padmāvatī immediately left, and after some time, they turned and looked back at Nabadwīp.

**144.** “They wept, and tears streamed from their eyes. Humbly, they spoke to the residents of Nabadwīp.

**145.** “‘Please be merciful to these two souls. Please forgive us for the offences we have committed.’

**146.** “They walked away, looking back again and again at the eight-petalled lotus of Nabadwīp.

**147.** “They went ahead and eventually did not see Nabadwīp anymore. Crying, they crossed outside the land of Gauḍa.

**148.** “Some time later, they arrived in Nīlāchal and saw Jagannāth with cheerful hearts.

**149.** “O Jīva, this is the place of Jayadev. The elders say that only this raised land is what remains of it.”

**150.** Seeing the place of Jayadev, Śrī Jīva rolled on the ground and wept in divine love.

**151.** “Glory to the poet Jayadev! Glory to Padmāvatī! Glory to *Śrī Gīta- govinda*! Glory to love for Kṛṣṇa!

**152.** “O Lord, mercifully give me a drop of the ocean of divine love that Jayadev relished.”

**153.** Saying this, Jīva tumbled to the ground and rolled at the holy feet of Nityānanda Prabhu.

**154.** That night they stayed at the house of Vāṇīnāth, and he and his family served Nityānanda Prabhu.

**155.** This poor, fallen soul, whose aspiration is the shade of Nitāi and Jāhnavā’s feet, chants the glories of Nadia.

---

# Chapter 12. Śrī Śrī Ṛtudwīp and Śrī Rādhā Kuṇḍa

**1.** All glory to the moon-like Śrī Chaitanya, Nityānanda Prabhu, Advaita, Gadādhar, Śrīvās, and all the devotees! All glory to the home of Jagannāth Miśra and the best of abodes, Śrī Nabadwīp!

**2.** At dawn, the devotees arose and called out to Śrī Gaura and Nitāichā̐d. Feeling great joy, Nityānanda and the devotees then departed, leaving Champā Haṭṭa behind.

**3.** From then on, Vāṇīnāth came with Nityānanda. He considered, “When else will I get the chance to joyfully circumambulate Māyāpur with Nitāichā̐d and go to the home of the Lord?”

**4.** Eventually, they came to Rātupur. Upon seeing the beauty of the town, Nityānanda Prabhu said, “We have come to Ṛtudwīp, which is extremely enchanting.

**5.** “The trees bow their heads, the breeze blows gently, and the flowers bloom in all directions. The humming of the bees and the fragrant nectar of the flowers intoxicate the hearts of travellers.”

**6.** As He spoke, Nityānanda Rāy became almost mad and said, “Quickly bring My horn! The calves have run off, and Kānāi is asleep. Like a child, He does not come now.

**7.** “Where are Subal and Śrīdām? I, Balarām, cannot go out to tend the cows alone.” With great power, Nityānanda called out, “Kānāi! Kānāi!” and jumped a couple of metres.

**8.** Seeing His ecstasy, the devotees quickly submitted at His feet, “O Nityānanda Prabhu! Your brother Gaurachandra is not here now.

**9.** “The Lord has taken sannyās and gone to Nīlāchal, leaving us destitute.” Hearing this, Nityānanda became dismayed. He fell to the ground and wept.

**10.** “What sorrow! Brother Kānāi! You left us and became a sannyāsī in Nīlāchal! I cannot continue this life; I will dive into the Yamunā.” Saying this, Nityānanda lost consciousness.

**11.** Everyone saw the intense ecstasy of Nityānanda and chanted the Lord’s Name. An hour and a half passed, but Nityānanda did not get up. Finally, the devotees sang songs about Gaurāṅga.

**12.** Hearing the Name of Gaurāṅga, Nitāi immediately arose and said, “This is the place of Rādhā Kuṇḍa. Here, Gaurahari would perform kīrtan with the devotees in the afternoon.

**13.** “Here, the six seasons meet and enhance the beauty of Gaurāṅga’s kīrtan Pastimes. Rādhā Kuṇḍa in Vraja is present here in Ṛtudwīp, where devotees drink and relish divine love.

**14.** “See also the beauty of Śyāma Kuṇḍa, which enchants the mind of every soul in this world, and the groves of the sakhīs nearby. Gorā would become immersed in saṅkīrtan here in the afternoon and satisfy everyone with the gift of divine love.

**15.** “Brother! We find no place like this in the three worlds. Know it to be a place for the devotees to serve. Souls who reside here attain the wealth of divine love, and their hearts become serene.”

**16.** Remaining there for the day, calling the Name of Śrī Gaurāṅga, all the devotees became immersed in divine love. They spent the day and night in Ṛtudwīp and worshipped the moon-like Śrī Chaitanya.

**17.** Then, dancing as He went, Nityānanda Prabhu arrived in Śrī Vidyā Nagar. The beauty of the town attracts the minds of sages, and upon seeing it, the devotees were jubilant.

**18.** The poor Bhakti Vinod, whose true wealth is the feet of Nitāi and Jāhnavā, chants the glories of Nadia. Grasping the feet of the devotees, he prays only for the wealth of devotion to Kṛṣṇa.

---

# Chapter 13. Śrī Vidyā Nagar and Śrī Jahnudwīp

**1.** All glory to Gaura, Nityānanda, Advaita, Gadādhar, Śrīvās, and the ocean of kīrtan, Śrī Nabadwīp Dhām!

**2.** Coming to Vidyā Nagar, Nityānanda Rāy taught Śrī Jīva the importance of the town.

**3.** “During the destruction of the universe, the eternal abode of Nabadwīp remains undisturbed as an eight-petalled lotus.

**4.** “All the Avatārs and thousands of fortunate souls stay in one part of that lotus.

**5.** “In Vidyā Nagar within Ṛtudwīp, the Lord, as Matsya, holds all the Vedas.

**6.** “The Vedas, which contain all knowledge, took shelter and stayed here. They named this place Śrī Vidyā Nagar.

**7.** “When Brahmā desired to create the world again, he saw the full devastation and became extremely afraid.

**8.** “At that time, the Lord bestowed mercy upon him. He received that mercy here and then prayed to the Lord.

**9.** “As he opened his mouths, the extremely beautiful goddess Saraswatī manifested from his tongues.

**10.** “Becoming empowered by Saraswatī, four-headed Brahmā prayed to Kṛṣṇa and felt great satisfaction.

**11.** “When creation takes place, Māyā remains on the bank of the Virajā River and envelops all directions with the three modes of material nature.

**12–13.** “The sages then reveal knowledge within that world manifested by Māyā. They take shelter in this abode of Saraswatī and conquer ignorance.

**14.** “They study the sixty-four branches of knowledge and then teach them at various places on the earth.

**15.** “The residences of all the sages who study each and every type of knowledge are present here eternally.

**16.** “Śrī Vālmīki came here by the grace of Nārad and learned the art of poetry.

**17.** “Dhanvantari came here and learned āyurveda. Viśvāmitra and others learned archery.

**18.** “Śaunaka and other sages studied Vedic mantras. The god of gods, Lord Śiva, studied tantra.

**19.** “At the request of the sages, the four Vedas manifested from the four mouths of Lord Brahmā.

**20.** “Kapila resided here and wrote about sāṅkhya, and Śrī Gautam Ṛṣi wrote texts about logic and argument.

**21.** “Kaṇāda wrote vaiśeṣika, and Pātañjali wrote texts on yoga.

**22.** “Jaiminī wrote texts about mīmāṁsā, and Vedavyās wrote the Purāṇas and other texts.

**23.** “Nārad and four other sages wrote the Pañcharātras and taught souls spiritual practices.

**24.** “In this garden, all the Upaniṣads worshipped Śrī Gaurāṅga for a long time.

**25.** “Invisibly, Śrī Gaura said to them, ‘The conception of formlessness has polluted your hearts.

**26–27.** “‘As scriptures, you will not attain Me, but when you take birth as My associates in My manifest Pastimes, you will see Me and loudly chant My glories.’

**28.** “Hearing this, the Upaniṣads became silent and secretly waited here.

**29.** “This glorious Age of Kali, in which Śrī Gaurāṅga has descended, is the best of all ages.

**30–33.** “Hearing that Gaurāṅgasundar would perform Pastimes of scholarship, Bṛhaspati and his associates took birth. As Vāsudev Sārvabhauma, Bṛhaspati endeavoured greatly to please Gaurāṅga. Knowing within his heart, ‘My Lord will perform Pastimes of scholarship in Nabadwīp’, and becoming detached, Bṛhaspati left the assembly of Indra with his associates. Joyfully, he and his associates took birth in various places.

**34.** “Bṛhaspati made a school in Vidyā Nagar and taught here as Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭāchārya.

**35.** “Thinking, ‘Later, I may sink into the net of knowledge and forget Gaurāṅga’, he performed a trick.

**36.** “Keeping his disciples in Nadia, he went elsewhere before the birth of Gaura.

**37.** “He thought, ‘If I am Gaura’s servant, then my Lord will mercifully bring me to His side.’

**38.** “Thinking this, Sārvabhauma went to Nīlāchal and propagated texts on māyāvād there.

**39.** “Here, during His Pastimes of scholarship, Gaurachandra Prabhu defeated and joked with the disciples of Sārvabhauma.

**40.** “Sometimes the Lord came to Vidyā Nagar, posed trick questions, and defeated everyone.

**41.** “Defeated, all the teachers and students would flee.

**42.** “Ignorance leaves those who hear about Gaurāṅga’s extraordinary Pastimes of scholarship.”

**43.** Hearing this with the joy of divine love, Jīva rolled on the ground at the school in Vidyā Nagar, filled with divine love.

**44.** At the holy feet of Nityānanda, he submitted, “I have a doubt. Please cut it away.

**45.** “Knowledge of analysis and argument are inauspicious, so how do they reside in the eternal Dhām?”

**46.** Hearing this, Nityānanda Prabhu embraced Jīva and affectionately said, “Hari! Haribol!

**47.** “Nothing is inauspicious in the holy abode of the Lord. Here, argument and analysis have no power of their own.

**48.** “Here, everything is dependent on devotion and serves devotion. As a result of their sins, the wicked think the opposite.

**49.** “Here, the goddess of devotion is supreme: everyone is her servant, and everything reveals her.

**50.** “Nabadwīp is the abode of the ninefold practice of devotion. Here, action and knowledge always serve devotion.

**51.** “The scriptures give misconceptions to those who are averse, and love for Kṛṣṇa to those who are gentle.

**52.** “Prauḍhā Māyā, a servant of Gaurāṅga, is the presiding goddess here. In every age, she stays here and serves Gaurāṅga.

**53.** “Māyā blinds those who are very sinful and inimical to Vaiṣṇavas, and subjects them to various miseries.

**54.** “All sin and karma are annihilated here. In the form of knowledge, Prauḍhā Māyā destroys karma.

**55.** “If, however, someone offends a Vaiṣṇava, Prauḍhā Māyā drives them further into the miseries of karma.

**56.** “Such sinners may study in Nadia, but they never attain the wealth of divine love for the feet of Kṛṣṇa.

**57.** “They attain only ignorance of true knowledge and do not see the glory of Śrī Gaurāṅga in Nadia.

**58.** “Thus, knowledge is not inauspicious; its shadow, ignorance, is inauspicious.

**59.** “O Jīva, you will reveal all this by the mercy of Gaurāṅga. By His desire, you will write scriptures about it.

**60.** “Actually, He will write scriptures through you. Now, let us go to the residence of Jahnu Muni.”

**61.** While speaking, they went to Jān Nagar and saw the beauty of the hermitage of Jahnu Muni.

**62.** Nityānanda Prabhu said, “This charming abode is known as Jahnudwīp and Bhadravan.

**63.** “Jahnu Muni performed austerities here and saw the golden form of Gaura.

**64.** “Once, when Jahnu Muni sat here to perform his evening meditation, His copper vessel fell into the swiftly flowing River Gaṅgā.

**65.** “The copper vessel fell into the river and floated away. Jahnu Muni then drank all the water of the Gaṅgā with his palm.

**66.** “Mahārāj Bhagīrath thought, ‘Where did the Gaṅgā go?’ Bewildered, he began to think over the situation.

**67.** “Understanding that Jahnu Muni had drunk all the water of the Gaṅgā, Mahārāj Bhagīrath became concerned.

**68.** Sober Mahārāj Bhagīrath worshipped the sage for some time, and eventually the Gaṅgā broke out of the sage’s body.

**69.** “Thereafter, she became known as Jāhnavī. The whole world now calls her ‘Jāhnavī’.

**70.** “Some time later, Gaṅgā’s son, Bhīṣmadev, met his grandfather Jahnu Muni here.

**71.** “Jahnu Muni honoured Bhīṣma and kept Bhīṣma at his home for a long time.

**72.** “Bhīṣma learned profound religious principles from Jahnu Muni and later taught the essence of them to Yudhiṣṭhir Mahārāj.

**73.** “Residing in Nabadwīp, Bhīṣma attained the wealth of devotion and became recognised as a Vaiṣṇava.

**74.** “Thus, Jahnudwīp is supremely purifying. Fortunate souls reside here eternally.”

**75.** That day Nityānanda and the devotees stayed at the home of a devotee in Jahnudwīp.

**76.** The next morning, Nityānanda Prabhu took the devotees and went to Modadrumadwīp.

**77.** Bhakti Vinod, whose pride is the feet of Jāhnavā and Nitāi, chants the glories of Nadia.

---

# Chapter 14. Śrī Modadrumadwīp and Śrī Rāma-Līlā

**1.** All glory to the life of the Pañcha Tattva, Śrī Gaurahari, and the best of all abodes, Śrī Nabadwīp!

**2.** Proceeding to Māmgāchhi Grām, Nityānanda Rāy said, “This is Modadrumadwīp. Ayodhyā is present here.

**3–4.** “In a previous manifestation of the universe, while He was residing in the forest, Rāma came here with Lakṣmaṇ and Sītā, built a cottage under a massive banyan tree, and lived here joyfully for some time.

**5.** “Seeing the lustre of Nabadwīp, Rāma smiled slightly.

**6.** “His lotus eyes, His beautiful bow in hand—how enchanting was His dark grass-green form!

**7.** “He wore the dress of a brahmachārī, and dreadlocks shone atop His head. Seeing Him captivated all living beings.

**8.** “Seeing His smiling face, Sītā Devī asked Śrī Rāma about the reason for His smile.

**9.** “Rāma replied, ‘O Sītā, listen! There is a very confidential answer.

**10.** “‘When the glorious Kali-yuga takes place, souls will see My golden form here in Nadia.

**11.** “‘I will soon take birth as Gaurāṅga from the womb of Śachī Devī in the home of Jagannāth Miśra.

**12.** “‘The fortunate souls will see My childhood Pastimes, and I will give them all the highest form of divine love.

**13.** “‘Dear Sītā, at that time, I will perform Pastimes of scholarship and reveal the glories of My Name.

**14.** “‘Later, I will take sannyās and go to Nīlāchal. My mother will embrace My wife and cry.’

**15.** “Hearing this, Sītā said, ‘O lotus-eyed Lord, why will You make Your mother cry?

**16.** “‘Why will You take sannyās and leave Your wife? What happiness is there in making Your wife unhappy? I do not understand.’

**17.** “Śrī Rāma answered, ‘My dear, You actually understand everything, but to teach the souls, You now act as if You were unaware.

**18.** “‘Loving devotion to Me is relished in two ways. O Sītā, please hear My words.

**19.** “‘The joy felt during union with Me is called *sambhoga*. The joy felt during separation from Me is called *vipralambha*.

**20.** “‘My devotees, My eternal associates, desire sambhoga, and by My grace, they experience vipralambha.

**21.** “‘Devotees know that the sadness felt for My sake during vipralambha is actually the greatest joy.

**22.** “‘When sambhoga occurs after vipralambha, the happiness felt is ten million times greater than before.

**23.** “‘Separation from Me is the cause of that happiness. Accept this as truth; the four Vedas affirm it.

**24.** “‘During My Pastimes as Śrī Gaurāṅga, Mother Kauśalyā, who is known in the Vedas as Aditi, will appear as Śachī Devī.

**25.** “‘You will serve Me as Viṣṇu Priyā. In separation from Me, You will make a Deity of My golden form.

**26.** “‘In separation from You, I will make a golden Sītā and worship You in Ayodhyā.

**27.** “‘In exchange, You will make a Deity of My golden form and worship Me in Nadia.

**28.** “‘O Sītā, these profound topics are very confidential. They will not be revealed to the public now.

**29.** “‘Nabadwīp is very dear to Me: neither Ayodhyā nor any other place is equal to it.

**30.** “‘O Sītā, when Kali-yuga arrives, this banyan tree, Rāma Vaṭa, will disappear but invisibly remain.’

**31.** “In this way Rāma, Sītā, and Lakṣmaṇ resided here for some time.

**32.** “Eventually, They went to the Daṇḍaka forest to perform Their Pastimes. See here the place of Rāma’s cottage.

**33.** “By the will of the Lord, Rāma’s friend Guhaka took birth here as the son of a brāhmaṇ.

**34.** “His name became Sadānanda Vipra Bhaṭṭāchārya. He knew nothing other than Rāma within the three worlds.

**35.** “On the day that our Lord took birth in Māyāpur, Sadānanda was present in the home of Jagannāth Miśra.

**36.** “At the time of the Lord’s birth, all the gods observed the child in the home of Jagannāth Miśra.

**37.** “The brāhmaṇ, a great devotee, recognised the gods and understood that our Lord had taken birth there.

**38.** “With great delight, the brāhmaṇ returned to his home. In meditation on his worshippable Lord, he saw Gaurāṅgasundar.

**39.** “Śrī Gaurāṅga Rāy was sitting on a throne, and Brahmā and the other gods were waving chāmaras beside Him.

**40.** “Then, the brāhmaṇ saw the dark, grass-green Rāmachandra and heroic Lakṣmaṇ, the abode of Ananta, nearby.

**41.** “On His left was Sītā, and His devotee Hanumān sat in front of Him. Seeing this, the brāhmaṇ understood the identity of the Lord.

**42.** “With great joy, the brāhmaṇ went to Māyāpur and joyfully saw Gaurāṅga to the satisfaction of his eyes.

**43.** “‘I am blessed! I am blessed!’ said the brāhmaṇ again and again. ‘Rāmachandra has appeared before me as Gaurāṅga!’

**44.** “Later, when the saṅkīrtan Pastimes of the Lord started, Sadānanda would dance and chant the Name of Gaura.

**45.** “O Jīva, here pure devotees see Śrī Bhāṇḍīravan.”

**46.** Hearing this and seeing the eternal abode, the devotees surrounded Nityānanda and danced.

**47.** Divine ecstasy manifested in the body of Śrī Jīva, and he exclaimed, “O Gaurāṅga!”

**48.** With a jubilant heart, Nityānanda stayed in that village in the home of Nārāyaṇī that day.

**49.** The perfectly pure and chaste Nārāyaṇī, the mother of Vyāsa, personally served the devotees.

**50.** The next day in the morning, the devotees walked some distance and peacefully entered Śrī Vaikuṇṭhapur.

**51.** To fulfil the order of Nitāi and Jāhnavā, this poor, lowly soul chants the glories of Nadia.

---

# Chapter 15. Śrī Vaikuṇṭhapur, Śrī Rudradwīp, and Śrī Pulina

**1.** All glory to Śrī Gaurāṅga, the Pañcha Tattva, and the abode of Śrī Gaurāṅga, Śrī Nabadwīp!

**2.** Arriving in Śrī Vaikuṇṭhapur, Nityānanda Prabhu smiled gently and spoke to Śrī Jīva.

**3.** “Please listen carefully. Here, on one side of the eight-petalled lotus of Nabadwīp, is Vaikuṇṭhapur.

**4.** “Śrī Vaikuṇṭha, the spiritual world, the abode of Nārāyaṇ, is situated beyond the Virajā River. This is its position.

**5.** “Māyā never enters there, where Nārāyaṇ is served by the śrī, bhū, and līlā energies.

**6.** “The effulgence of this spiritual land is Brahma. With fleshy eyes, everyone sees it to be material.

**7.** “Nārad once saw this pure, eternal abode of Nārāyaṇ with his spiritual eyes.

**8.** “He saw Nārāyaṇ, and then saw Him as Gaurāṅgasundar. Thereafter, Nārad, the best of the sages, stayed here for some time.

**9.** “There is another ancient, confidential subject. Once, Rāmānuja Āchārya came to Jagannāth Kṣetra.

**10.** “He pleased Lord Jagannāth with numerous prayers, and Jagannāth mercifully came before him.

**11.** “Coming before him, the Lord said, ‘Visit Nabadwīp Dhām.

**12.** “‘Very soon, I will appear in Nadia in the home of Jagannāth Miśra.

**13.** “‘Nabadwīp is My dearmost abode. The spiritual world of Vaikuṇṭha is situated within one portion of it.

**14.** “‘You are My eternal servant, and foremost amongst My devotees. You must see Nabadwīp Dhām.

**15.** “‘Let your disciples remain here immersed in dāsya-rasa. You must go.

**16.** “‘O wise Rāmānuja! The birth of those who attain a body but do not see Nabadwīp is meaningless.

**17.** “‘Śrī Raṅgam, Śrī Veṅkaṭa, and Yādavāchal are simply minor parts of Nabadwīp.

**18.** “‘Therefore, O Rāmānuja, go to Nabadwīp and see the form of Gaurāṅga.

**19.** “‘You came to the earth to preach devotion. May your birth be successful by the mercy of Gaura.

**20.** “‘See Nabadwīp and then go to Kūrma Sthān. You will meet your disciples there.’

**21.** “Hearing this, Rāmānuja Āchārya joined his palms and prayed to Jagannāth.

**22.** “‘O Lord, by Your mercy I have now heard about Gaurāṅga, but I do not know who He is.’

**23.** “Jagannāth mercifully replied to Rāmānuja, ‘Everyone knows that Kṛṣṇa is the Lord of Goloka.

**24.** “‘Lord Nārāyaṇ is a form Kṛṣṇa assumes to perform Pastimes. Kṛṣṇa is the supreme being, and His abode is Vṛndāvan.

**25.** “‘Kṛṣṇa also exists fully and eternally as Gaurahari, and Vṛndāvan also exists fully and eternally as Nabadwīp.

**26.** “‘In Nabadwīp, I am eternally present as Gaurāṅgasundar. Nabadwīp is the best abode in the world.

**27.** “‘By My grace, Nabadwīp Dhām is present on the earth, and all the scriptures say that there is no trace of māyā there.

**28.** “‘If you think that Nabadwīp is degraded because it is present on the earth, then your devotion will diminish day by day.

**29.** “‘By My will, My inconceivable energy has manifested this spiritual abode within the realm of Māyā.

**30.** “The scriptures do not ascertain truth that is above reason. Only devotees understand it, by My grace.’

**31.** “Hearing the words of Jagannāth, the sober Rāmānuja became overwhelmed with divine love for Śrī Gaurāṅga.

**32.** “He said, ‘O Lord, Your Pastimes are extremely astonishing. The Vedic scriptures do not know Your glories.

**33.** “‘O Lord Jagannāth, why did the scriptures not specifically describe the Pastimes of Śrī Gaurāṅga?

**34.** “‘Only when I look at the Vedas, Purāṇas, and other scriptures in a deeper way do I find revelation of Gaurāṅga.

**35.** “‘Receiving Your order, I have left behind my doubts. The ecstasy of the Pastimes of Gaura has arisen in my heart.

**36.** “‘If You order me, I will go to Nabadwīp and then preach about the Pastimes of Gaura throughout the three worlds.

**37.** “‘I will reveal hidden scriptures, enlighten everyone, and make every soul within the three worlds a devotee of Gaura! Please speak.’

**38.** “Seeing the eagerness of Rāmānuja, Jagannāth said, ‘O Rāmānuja, do not speak this way.

**39.** “‘My Pastimes as Gaura are very confidential; keep them secret. Only after they are unmanifest will everyone receive them.

**40.** “‘Preach dāsya-rasa, and always worship Gaura within your heart.’

**41.** “Receiving this instruction, Rāmānuja Āchārya secretly came to Nabadwīp.

**42–43.** “So that the Pastimes of Gaura were not manifest prematurely, Nārāyaṇ took Rāmānuja and kept him in Śrī Vaikuṇṭhapur. Seeing this place, Rāmānuja was charmed.

**44.** “Nārāyaṇ, who is served by His śrī, bhū, and līlā energies, then very mercifully revealed Himself to Rāmānuja.

**45.** “Seeing His worshippable Lord, Rāmānuja felt himself to be most fortunate.

**46.** “Rāmānuja then suddenly saw the beautiful, enchanting, golden form of the son of Jagannāth Miśra.

**47.** “Rāmānuja fainted in the lustre of the Lord’s form, and Śrī Gaura put His feet on Rāmānuja’s head.

**48.** “With divine knowledge, Rāmānuja then prayed, ‘May I behold Your manifest Pastimes in Nadia.’

**49.** “Saying this, Rāmānuja cried with divine love. Then he said, ‘I will not leave Nabadwīp.’

**50.** “Mercifully, Gaurahari said, ‘O Rāmānuja, your desires will be fulfilled.

**51.** “‘When My Pastimes in Nadia manifest, you will be born again in Nabadwīp.’

**52.** “Saying this, Gaurahari disappeared. Satisfied, Rāmānuja departed.

**53.** “Eventually, Rāmānuja arrived in Kūrma Sthān and met his disci - ples there.

**54.** “He proceeded south and preached about dāsya-rasa while thinking of Nabadwīp and Śrī Gaurāṅga within His heart.

**55.** “By the mercy of Gaurāṅga, Rāmānuja took birth in this eternal abode as Śrī Ananta Āchārya.”

**56.** The party then went to the home of Vallabha Āchārya and saw the site of Lakṣmī and Gaurāṅga’s marriage.

**57.** “O devotees, see the site of Ananta Āchārya’s home. Here, there were many devotees of Nārāyaṇ.

**58.** “Everyone knows that the king of that time established worship of Nārāyaṇ in this place.”

**59.** Seeing the Niḥśreyasa forest on the bank of the Virajā River, the devotees felt unlimited joy.

**60.** While discussing this history, the devotees arrived in Mahatpur.

**61.** Nityānanda Prabhu said, “Kāmyavan is present here. Visit it with great devotion.

**62.** “Previously, there were five banyan trees here, but by the will of the Lord, they have since disappeared.

**63.** “This place is now called Mātāpur. The scriptures state that its previous name was Mahatpur.

**64.** “Once, the five Pāṇḍavas came to Gauḍa with Draupadī to live incognito.

**65.** “Understanding the glories of Nadia through a dream in Ekachakrā, Mahārāj Yudhiṣṭhir became amazed.

**66.** “Desiring to see Nabadwīp, the Pāṇḍavas came here the next day with great joy.

**67.** “Seeing the beauty of Nabadwīp, the Pāṇḍavas praised the fortune of the residents of Gauḍa.

**68.** “They stayed here for some time and killed various demons.

**69.** “Everyone, see there Yudhiṣṭhir Ṭilā. See here the pond of Draupadī.

**70.** “Understanding the glories of this place, Mahārāj Yudhiṣṭhir stayed here for some time.

**71.** “One day, in a dream, he saw the extraordinary form of Gaurāṅga illuminate all directions.

**72.** “Smiling, Gaurāṅga said, ‘Behold this very secret form.

**73.** “‘I am Kṛṣṇa, the son of Nanda, and I always stay in your house as your friendly companion.

**74.** “‘Nabadwīp Dhām is the best of all abodes. It will manifest during Kali-yuga and dispel all darkness.

**75.** “‘You are all My eternal servants, and when I appear, you will also take birth.

**76.** “‘I will live with you permanently in Puruṣottam Kṣetra on the bank of the ocean in Odisha.

**77.** “‘From here, go to Odisha, purify the country, and remove the sorrow of the people.’

**78.** “After having this dream, Yudhi ṣṭ hir spoke to his brothers. They consulted with Draupadī and then went to Odisha.

**79.** “They felt great sorrow to leave Nabadwīp. Still, they followed the order of the Lord.

**80.** “Madhva Āchārya also stayed here with his disciples for some time as a resident of the Dhām.

**81.** “Gaurāṅgasundar mercifully revealed His most enchanting form to Madhva Āchārya in a dream.

**82.** “Smiling, Gaurachandra said to Madhva Āchārya, ‘Everyone knows that you are My eternal servant.

**83.** “‘When I appear in Nabadwīp, I will follow your lineage.

**84–85.** “‘Now, carefully eradicate illusionism and false scriptures from all regions, and establish the glory of the Lord’s Deity form. Later, I will develop your pure conception.’

**86.** “Saying this, Gaurachandra disappeared. Madhva Āchārya awoke and then fainted.

**87.** “Madhva Āchārya then cried, wondering, ‘Will I ever see that beautiful golden form again?’

**88.** “Then a divine voice came from the clear sky, ‘Serve Me secretly, and you will come to Me.’

**89.** “Madhva Āchārya became resolute and defeated the māyāvādīs in all directions.”

**90.** As Nityānanda Prabhu spoke about all this history, the devotees suddenly arrived in Rudradwīp.

**91.** Nityānanda Prabhu said, “This is Rudradwīp. It has been divided into two parts by the Gaṅgā.

**92.** “By the will of the Lord, people do not live here. See how this western island has moved to the east bank of the river.

**93.** “From here, see Śrī Śaṅkarpur. See how far it shines along the bank of the Gaṅgā.

**94.** “When Śaṅkar Āchārya was conquering all directions, he came there to conquer Nabadwīp.

**95.** “At heart, Śaṅkar Āchārya was a great Vaiṣṇava; only externally was he acting as a nondualist and servant of Māyā.

**96.** “He was a highly empowered manifestation of Śiva who was expert at preaching a conception of veiled Buddhism.

**97–98.** “According to the order of the Lord, Śiva performs this duty. When Śaṅkar Āchārya came to Nadia, Gaurachandra revealed Himself to Śaṅkar Āchārya in a dream and mercifully spoke sweet words to him.

**99.** “‘You are My servant, and following My order, you are very cleverly preaching illusionism.

**100.** “‘Nabadwīp Dhām is very dear to Me, and illusionism will never have a place here.

**101.** “‘Here, Vṛddha Śiva and Prauḍhā Māyā propagate imaginary interpretations of the scriptures.

**102.** “‘They deceive, however, only those who oppose My devotees.

**103.** “‘In general, My devotees reside here. This place is not for preaching misconception.

**104.** “‘Therefore, you should go elsewhere. Do not trouble the residents of Nabadwīp.’

**105.** “Understanding the position of Nabadwīp through this dream, Śaṅkar Āchārya went elsewhere, inspired with devotion.

**106.** “The eleven Rudras reside here in Rudradwīp and chant the glories of Gaura.

**107.** “Śrī Nīlalohita Śiva, the leader of the Nīlalohita Rudras, always dances here with great joy.

**108.** “Seeing the dancing of the Rudras, the gods in the sky joyfully shower flowers upon them.

**109.** “Once, Viṣṇu Swāmī, while conquering all directions, came to Rudradwīp with his disciples and stayed the night.

**110.** “The disciples danced, chanting, ‘Hari! Hari!’, and Viṣṇu Swāmī recited prayers from the scriptures.

**111.** “Seeing this devotional discussion, Śrī Nīlalohita Śiva was pleased and mercifully revealed himself.

**112.** “Upon seeing that Śiva had arrived at a Vaiṣṇava assembly, Viṣṇu Swāmī became completely amazed.

**113.** “He immediately joined his palms and prayed to Śiva. Melting with compassion, Śiva spoke to him.

**114.** “‘All you Vaiṣṇavas are very dear to me, and my heart is delighted to see your discussion of devotion.

**115.** “‘Request a boon, and I will kindly give it to you. There is nothing that I do not give to Vaiṣṇavas.’

**116.** “Filled with divine love, Viṣṇu Swāmī bowed down, joined his palms, and requested a boon.

**117.** “‘O Lord, give us the boon that hereafter we will establish a school of devotion.’

**118.** “With great joy, Rudra granted this boon and named the school after himself.

**119.** “Viṣṇu Swāmī thus named his school the Śrī Rudra sampradāya, and danced and sang.

**120.** “By Śiva’s mercy, Viṣṇu Swāmī stayed here and worshipped Śrī Gaurachandra, aspiring for divine love.

**121.** “Śrī Gaurāṅga came to Viṣṇu Swāmī in a dream and said, ‘My devotee Śiva blessed you.

**122.** “‘You are fortunate to have attained the wealth of devotion in Nabadwīp. For now, preach pure nondualism.

**123.** “‘Later, at the time of My appearance, you will appear as Śrī Vallabha Bhaṭṭa.

**124.** “‘You will meet Me in Śrī Kṣetra, later go to Mahāvan, and establish your school.’

**125.** “O Jīva, Śrī Vallabha is now in Gokula. When you go there, you will meet him.”

**126.** Saying this, Nityānanda happily walked southbound towards Pāraḍāṅgā through Śrī Pulina.

**127.** Passing through Śrī Pulina, Nityānanda Prabhu showed the devotees Śrī Rāsa Maṇḍal and Dhīra Samīra.

**128.** He said, “O Jīva, see here eternal Vṛndāvan and its Pastimes.”

**129.** Hearing of Vṛndāvan, Jīva became overwhelmed with divine love, and tears of love streamed from his eyes.

**130.** Nityānanda Prabhu said, “Śrī Gaurāṅga chanted verses about the Rāsa-līlā here with the devotees.

**131.** “O Jīva, this place on the bank of the Gaṅgā is the place of the great Rāsa-līlā in Vṛndāvan.

**132.** “Daily, the Rāsa-līlā happens here with the gopīs. Sometimes fortunate souls see this.

**133.** “See Śrī Dhīra Samīra to the west. O wise one, hear about this place of worship.

**134.** “The place known as Dhīra Samīra on the bank of the Yamunā in Vraja is present here on the bank of the Gaṅgā.

**135.** “Actually, you do not see the bank of the Gaṅgā here. The Yamunā flows on the west side of the Gaṅgā.

**136.** “This beautiful bank is on the shore of the Yamunā. Thus, Viśvambhar called this place Vṛndāvan.

**137.** “O Jīva, know that all the places of the Pastimes in Vṛndāvan are present here.

**138.** “There is no difference between Vṛndāvan and Nabadwīp, and one should never differentiate between Gaura and Kṛṣṇa.”

**139.** Exuberant with intense ecstasy, Nityānanda Rāy revealed Vṛndāvan to Jīva and then took him ahead.

**140.** They went a little ways north and spent that night in Rudradwīp.

**141.** Bhakti Vinod, whose wealth is the feet of Nitāi and Jāhnavā, chants the glories of Nadia.

---

# Chapter 16. Śrī Bilvapakṣa and Śrī Bharadvāja Ṭilā

**1.** All glory to the reveller of Nadia, Gaurachandra! All glory to the Lord of Ekachakrā, Nityānanda Prabhu!

**2.** All glory to the Lord of Śāntipur, Advaita Āchārya, and the resident of Rāmachandrapur Gadādhar Paṇḍit!

**3.** All glory to the land of Gauḍa, made of pure spiritual gemstone, where Kṛṣṇa performed Pastimes during Kali-yuga!

**4.** Nityānanda Prabhu crossed the Gaṅgā, went a little ways ahead, and said, “O devotees, look!

**5.** “This enchanting place is named Bilvapakṣa. Everyone calls it Bel Pukhariyā.

**6.** “See here in Nabadwīp the place which the scriptures call Bilvavan in Vraja Dhām.

**7–8.** “Śiva, the lord of bael, resides here. After a group of noble brāhmaṇs satisfied him, having worshipped him with bael leaves for a fortnight, Śiva gave them the boon of devotion to Kṛṣṇa.

**9.** “Amongst the br ā hma ṇ s was Nimb ā ditya, who worshipped Ś iva exceptionally well.

**10.** “Mercifully, Śiva said to him, ‘On the edge of this village is a divine bael forest.

**11.** “‘The four Kumāras are meditating in that forest, and by their grace, you will attain divine knowledge.

**12.** “‘The four Kumāras are your Gurus, and through their service, you will attain everything here.’

**13.** “Saying this, Śiva disappeared. Nimbāditya then searched and found the place.

**14–16.** “In the bael forest, he saw the four Kumāras—the sages Sanak, Sananda, Sanātan, and Sanat Kumār—seated on a beautiful platform beside Śiva, unseen by others. They were unclothed, very young, and of noble character.

**17.** “Greatly excited upon seeing them, Nimbāditya Āchārya happily called out, ‘Hare Kṛṣṇa! Hare Kṛṣṇa!’

**18.** “Hearing the Name of the Lord, their meditation broke, and they saw the form of a Vaiṣṇava in front of them.

**19.** “Seeing the Vaiṣṇava, they were pleased, and one by one they embraced Nimbāditya.

**20.** “‘Who are you? Why have you come here? Please introduce yourself. We will certainly fulfil your prayers.’

**21.** “Hearing their words, Nimbāditya prostrated and humbly introduced himself.

**22.** “Upon hearing Nimbārka’s introduction, Śrī Sanat Kumār spoke with a smiling face.

**23.** “‘Knowing that Kali-yuga would be dark, the merciful Lord decided to preach devotion.

**24.** “‘He empowered four devotees and sent them to preach devotion throughout the world.
